Banking and card acquiring in Estonia, set beside Cyprus

What a new private limited company (OÜ) in Estonia meets on the way from formation to its first card payment, by the same eight questions as the Cyprus page: documents by stage, agreements with banks and payment providers, entry points, an online and an offline example, laws and supervisors, taxes, KYC, and costs. One more question runs through it: what changes when the founder does not live in Estonia. The first section sets Estonia beside Cyprus row by row.

Reference study, not legal or tax advice. Every statement carries its status as read on 5 October 2026 and a number that leads to the record behind it and to the document that record was read in. Prices, product availability and statements that something is not published hold for that date only; the last section lists what was not established and when to look again.

gid research round estonia-banking-acquiring · 5 October 2026 · 590 records in memory, 94.1% read in the primary document · seven research slices, each checked by two adversarial verifiers: 237 of 490 facts corrected, none refuted, 108 added · the page was written from the records on 5 October; every item was checked against them (one section on 5 October, the rest on 8 October 2026): 218 of 555 items corrected, none removed, 54 added

Side by sideCyprus and Estonia, row by row: formation, accounts, acquiring, taxesEach row states each country's side as that country's own records state it: the value, the instrument and the date. Four parts: formation, yearly duties and the founder abroad; bank accounts and KYC; acquiring and providers; taxes. Read in the first week of October 2026.
  2. Question 1An OÜ is founded online in one application only if every signer holds an Estonian means of signingAs of 5 October 2026 a private limited company (osaühing, OÜ) has no minimum share capital. On an expedited-procedure application the state fee is EUR 265 and by law review is due no later than the next working day; otherwise the founding documents go through a notary. The annual report is due within six months of the end of the financial year.
  3. Question 1, continuedFounder abroad: no residence condition in the Code's OÜ part; only Estonian tools sign on the portalThe Commercial Code's private limited company (OÜ) part sets no residence or nationality condition for a founder or board member (5 October 2026). Signing: an Estonian eID such as the e-resident's digital ID (EUR 150, collected in person, closed to twelve states' citizens save exceptions) or a notary. Board fees are taxed in Estonia wherever members live.
  4. Question 2Swedbank, SEB and LHV sign the merchant contract themselves; Coop Pank works through partnersOn 5 October 2026 Swedbank, SEB and LHV are themselves the contracting party for terminals; Coop Pank works with Nets and Montonio. Stripe, Adyen, PayPal, Wise, myPOS and Viva contract under another state's law; SumUp's Estonian terms choose Estonian law. Montonio's merchant terms were not among its published documents; Revolut's live pages were not read.
  5. Question 3Swedbank, SEB and LHV sign merchants themselves; Coop Pank works through Nets and MontonioSwedbank, SEB and LHV are the acquiring banks at the terminal and behind the EveryPay gateway online; Coop Pank refers merchants to Nets and Montonio; Luminor is the acquirer named in Maksekeskus's terms. On 5 October 2026 the supervisor's register also lists eleven Estonian payment and four e-money institutions, and several hundred passported entries.
  6. Question 4Two outlines: an online shop whose board member lives abroad, and a café in TallinnBoth walk-throughs are outlines assembled from the records, with prices as of 5 October 2026. In the portal the shop's founding application can be signed only with an Estonian ID-card (the e-resident's card included), Mobile-ID or Smart-ID. An ordinary café notifies instead of obtaining a food licence; a cash register is required once it sells alcohol.
  7. Question 5Finantsinspektsioon and the Financial Intelligence Unit over providers; consumer law over merchantsFinantsinspektsioon licenses payment and e-money institutions and supervises banks and financial institutions under it; the Financial Intelligence Unit is the default anti-money-laundering supervisor. Merchant rules - no surcharge on consumer cards, cash accepted and rounded, a purchase document - sit in the obligations, euro and consumer protection acts.
  8. Question 6Profit is taxed when paid out, at 22/78; pay bears 33% social tax; standard VAT is 24%As a rule an Estonian company pays no income tax on profit it keeps: tax falls on distributions, declared monthly. Nothing is withheld from a dividend except a 7% transitional remnant. Two income tax rises enacted for 2026 were cancelled before they applied, and no income tax rate change is enacted for 2027 as of 5 October 2026.
  9. Question 7KYC: the act lists what a bank must establish; the 'connection with Estonia' test is not in the actThe anti-money-laundering act makes an institution identify the customer, its representative and its beneficial owner and understand the purpose of the relationship; the phrase 'connection with Estonia' is not in it. Each of the four banks read on 5 October 2026 publishes such a test, and a non-refundable fee for a company with a non-resident owner.
  10. Question 8State fee EUR 265 expedited, EUR 200 otherwise; the Accounting Act requires no accountantAs of 5 October 2026 the state fee for entering a private limited company (OÜ) is EUR 200, or EUR 265 on an expedited-procedure application; texts published up to 1 July 2027 keep both. A notary, contact person and auditor are required only in stated cases; the Accounting Act requires no accountant. Vendors' prices are their own claims.
  11. 2015–20262015 to 2026 and the dates already fixed: a bank clean-up, tax rises, a new register actEach event carries the date of effect where a record gives one and names the act or the body. Entries dated after 5 October 2026 are dates set in adopted acts, EU regulations, published notices or bills still before the Riigikogu (the parliament); a proposed date is marked as proposed. Events resting on press reports or on a firm's own notice say so.
  12. Not establishedWhat the records leave open on 5 October 2026, and when each statement goes staleQuestions the Estonian records leave open or only narrow. Each item says what the records establish, where it was looked for, what would close it and when to look again. AML stands for anti-money-laundering, EEA for the European Economic Area, ECB for the European Central Bank, OÜ for a private limited company, eID for an electronic identity.
Side by side · Cyprus and Estonia, row by row

Cyprus and Estonia, row by row: formation, accounts, acquiring, taxes

Each row states each country's side as that country's own records state it: the value, the instrument and the date. Four parts: formation, yearly duties and the founder abroad; bank accounts and KYC; acquiring and providers; taxes. Read in the first week of October 2026.

Formation, upkeep and the founder abroad: an advocate in Cyprus, an Estonian e-signature in Estonia

Part 1, 4-5 October 2026. In Cyprus an advocate drafts the founding documents; accounts are audited or, below set limits, reviewed. An Estonian OÜ can be founded online if every signer holds an Estonian e-identity; review or audit starts above size limits. In the provisions read, neither company law sets a residence or nationality condition for directors.

Formation

WhatCyprusEstonia
Minimum share capitalNone for a private company, per the Registrar of Companies (4 October 2026); Cap. 113, s. 4A(1) sets a minimum only for public companies.None for a private limited company (osaühing, OÜ) since 1 February 2023 (Commercial Code § 136); until 31 January 2023, at least €2,500. Since 1 February 2023 founders pay for their shares in full before the registration application is filed (§ 140(2)). Text in force 5 October 2026.123
Who drafts the founding documentsOnly a practising advocate, who certifies on the documents that he drafted them; documents drafted in breach are void (Advocates Law, Cap. 2, s. 11(5), in force 4 October 2026).Expedited procedure: standard articles laid down by ministerial regulation, digitally signed by all founders, with no provisions of the company's own (Commercial Code § 139¹). Otherwise a notary authenticates the memorandum and articles, or a sole founder's resolution (§ 138(4)-(5)). In force 5 October 2026.456
Name step; the register's feeName approval first, €10 per proposed name; registration €165 in total with share capital (Registrar, 4 October 2026).No compulsory name approval; entry €200, or €265 on an expedited-procedure application (State Fees Act § 62(2), since 1 January 2022).7891011
Time limit in the law (Cyprus: to complete formation online; Estonia: to review an application); not the time achievedOnly the online route has one: formation completed within 5 working days where only natural persons form the company on the Registrar's templates, 10 otherwise, counted from the later of completing the formalities and paying the fee (Cap. 113, s. 366Ε(5), from Law 151(I)/2023).Review within five working days of arrival, restarting when the application is amended; the registrar may extend it up to three months for special verification. Expedited procedure: no later than the next working day (Commercial Register Act §§ 41(3)-(5), 42(2), in force 5 October 2026).1213
Officers; residence or nationality conditionAt least one director and a secretary; a sole director may also be the secretary only in a single-member private company (Cap. 113, ss. 170, 171(1)). Ss. 170-172, read 4 October 2026, contain no residence or nationality requirement for either.A management board of one or more natural persons, who need not be shareholders; no company secretary (Commercial Code § 180). What is issued on registrationA certificate of incorporation. Audit or review of the annual accountsAudit for every private company (Cap. 113, s. 152A(1)(a)); instead, a review by a statutory auditor where net turnover and total assets stay within €300,000 and €500,000 for at least two consecutive financial years. €300,000 since 6 February 2026; per ICPAC, the accountants' institute, for periods beginning on or after it.Review where two of revenue €2,000,000, assets €1,000,000, 24 employees on average, or one of €6,000,000, €3,000,000, 72, are exceeded (Auditors Activities Act § 92); audit where two of €5,000,000, €2,500,000, 50, or one of €15,000,000, €7,500,000, 180, are (§ 91). Source trap

€165 and €265 do not buy the same path (fees as of 4-5 October 2026). Cyprus: €165 is the Registrar's fee with share capital; each proposed name costs €10, and an advocate must draft the documents. Estonia: €265 is the expedited-procedure fee; the ordinary entry is €200, and there the founding documents go through a notary, whose fee under the Notary Fees Act comes on top, VAT added. A founder who needs the e-resident's card pays a further €150 state fee.4679116073

Source trap

No residence condition is not the same as filing from abroad. Estonia: per the Centre of Registers (12 November 2025), another EU state's eID enters the register portal 'to view the data'; statements and reports are signed only with Estonian tools, e-residency card included. A foreign qualified signature was not tested. Cyprus: from 1 July 2026 a company's Tax Register request goes only through Tax For All via CY Login, whose profile is not said; a company's own profile is verified only in person.1963747576

Source trap

Exceptions differ by citizenship (Regulation No 28, § 2). For ten of the twelve states, one is at least three consecutive years' residence in an EEA state, the UK or Switzerland just before applying, on that state's valid permit; for Russians and Belarusians the only one is an earlier card plus permanent economic activity in Estonia. The Government's reason (bill 572 SE, adopted 4 June 2025): background checks alone do not reduce risk enough where Estonia lacks judicial, security or law-enforcement cooperation.777879

Source trap

The identities that sign on the Estonian register portal have narrowed. Archived snapshots of the Centre of Registers page: 5 January 2016, an Estonian, Portuguese, Belgian, Finnish or Lithuanian ID card; 18 August 2019, an Estonian, Latvian, Belgian or Finnish ID card or Estonian or Lithuanian Mobile ID; 2 April 2023, only an Estonian ID-card or Mobile ID. Its help page of 12 November 2025: an Estonian ID-card (e-residency card included), Smart-ID or Mobile-ID. The date and instrument of the change were not found.8081

Source trap

Who sees owner data differs, and both countries are changing it. Cyprus: the general public's access has been suspended since 23 November 2022; Law 25(I)/2026 harmonises with Art. 74 of Directive (EU) 2024/1640, and no procedure for it was found on 4 October 2026. Estonia: on 8 June 2026 the Ministry of Finance, citing the EU's new anti-money-laundering framework and Court of Justice case law, announced a restriction from 10 July 2026; it was not applied, and no new date was published by 5 October 2026.31323682

Bank account and AML: Cyprus's one directive for all customers, Estonian banks' 'connection' test

Part 2, as of 4-5 October 2026. The Central Bank of Cyprus directive has no separate regime for non-residents. Estonia's anti-money-laundering (AML) act sets no 'connection with Estonia' test; its supervisor's guidelines list a missing one as a risk and the four banks read ask for one. In both countries a company has no right to a basic payment account.

Bank account

WhatCyprusEstonia
Non-resident owner: the ruleThe Central Bank of Cyprus (CBC) directive of 2025 (K.D.P. 120/2025, from 2 June 2025) has no separate regime for non-resident customers; residence is a risk factor and, where the customer or its beneficial owner resides in a high-risk third country, an enhanced-measures trigger (paras 12(6), 50(3)-(4)).The AML act (RahaPTS) nowhere says 'connection with Estonia' (searched 5 October 2026); Finantsinspektsioon's advisory guidelines (from 1 May 2024) list 'no connection with Estonia' as a payment-service risk. In outline, LHV, Swedbank, SEB and Coop Pank ask a company with a non-resident owner or board member to document one.89909192
Documents that identify a company, in the ruleCBC directive, para 37(10): among others the certificates of incorporation, registered office, directors and secretary and (private companies) shareholders, memorandum and articles, a board resolution, and recent audited financial statements or, failing those, management accounts; foreign companies supply equivalent documents.RahaPTS § 22 (text in force from 21 July 2026): the registry card or registration certificate of the register, or an equivalent document, which 'does not need to be demanded from the customer' where the bank has access to the commercial register or the relevant foreign register.9394
Fee at opening, company with a foreign link (see the note)Bank of Cyprus (June 2026): EUR 300 for entities incorporated outside both the EEA and its twelve 'third equivalent countries', EUR 500 for those requiring enhanced due diligence (new customers). Eurobank Limited (3 November 2025): assessment, minimum EUR 200. Alpha Bank Cyprus (6 April 2026): organisations, minimum EUR 500.Not refunded on refusal: LHV EUR 300 (01.05.2024), Swedbank EUR 300 (01.11.2023), SEB EUR 500 (01.06.2026; EUR 0 until 30 November 2026 on its offers page for 'non-resident shareholders', non-EEA residents excluded in the Estonian text), Coop Pank EUR 200 or EUR 500 by the country behind the ownership (1 August 2026).9596979899100101102103

E-money institutions are not named (5 October 2026).105
Basic payment accountFor consumers legally resident in the EU; legal entities have no such right (Law 64(I)/2017). Deposit guaranteeUp to EUR 100,000 per depositor per credit institution, accrued interest included (CBC, 4 October 2026). Register of bank accountsKept by the CBC (Law 188(I)/2007, art. 61Δ, inserted by Law 13(I)/2021 of 23 February 2021; in force on 4 October 2026, date it became operational not established); the financial intelligence unit (MOKAS), the Police, the Tax Department and the Customs Department may search it directly.The Enforcement Register (controller: Ministry of Justice and Digital Affairs), since 1 January 2024, under RahaPTS § 81 (duty applied since 10 September 2020); 'a holder of public authority' with a statutory task may join (Code of Enforcement Procedure § 63(2)). Meeting the applicant from abroad, per the banks' pages (read 4-5 October 2026)Bank of Cyprus: a visit to one of its twelve International Business Units in person, or an application through a local professional intermediary. AML and KYC

WhatCyprusEstonia
AML supervisor of banks; its rulebook (binding beside advisory)The CBC, under Law 188(I)/2007 (in force since 1 January 2008); its directive K.D.P. 120/2025 is binding (art. 59(4)).Finantsinspektsioon, under the Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Prevention Act (RahaPTS, in force from 27 November 2017, § 64); its Advisory Guidelines, valid from 1 May 2024, are comply-or-explain.91124125126127128

Identification without a meeting, in the lawElectronic identification under Regulation (EU) No 910/2014 or another secure remote process accepted by a competent authority of the Republic (Law, art. 61(1)(a)); a remote start is not high-risk by default (CBC directive, para 27).RahaPTS § 31 (from 1 July 2024): for a customer not met in person who lives or is seated outside the EEA, or whose outgoing payments exceed EUR 15,000 a month (legal person EUR 25,000), an eIDAS-notified scheme of level substantial or high or a qualified trust service; if that is not possible, a recorded video procedure.131132133
Beneficial-owner threshold25% plus one share, or an ownership interest above 25%, is an 'indication' of ownership (Law 188(I)/2007, art. 2(1)). Regulation (EU) 2024/1624 applies here too from 10 July 2027.134135136137
What a 'high-risk third country' isA country on the EU list (26 jurisdictions on 4 October 2026) 'and a third country which obliged entities classify as high-risk' (Law 188(I)/2007, art. 2).A country on the Commission's list (RahaPTS § 3 clause 18; the Act holds no national list); § 37(4) adds geographic risk factors. An appendix to the Financial Intelligence Unit's guidelines, dated 18 September 2024 and still the linked version on 5 October 2026, lists 32 jurisdictions with a higher risk of terrorist financing.138139140141
Excluded countries or citizenships in banks' published textsBank of Cyprus (read 4 October 2026): products 'are not available to customers in certain countries'; its account-opening page publishes no list. Eurobank Limited's international account page and fee table name no excluded countries (same day).LHV (undated FAQ, read 5 October 2026): stopped onboarding companies whose beneficial owners are Russian or Belarusian citizens 'without a valid Estonian residence permit'. Dated steps since 20182 November 2018: CBC circular (archived copy) that relationships with shell companies meeting listed conditions 'shall be avoided'; the February 2019 directive revoked and absorbed it. 16 November 2023: the CBC stated that since end-2018 credit institutions had ended relationships with 42,728 shell companies; it gave no method.26 March 2018: the European Central Bank withdrew Versobank AS's licence, above all for money-laundering-prevention breaches. 19 February 2019: Finantsinspektsioon's precept barring Danske Bank A/S's Estonian branch from operating (its non-resident business forced to end in 2015). 1 November 2023: RahaPTS fines raised.145146147148149
Enhanced due diligence the law makes mandatoryLaw 188(I)/2007 art. 64 (in force on 4 October 2026): business with high-risk third countries, cross-border correspondent relationships and politically exposed persons. The CBC directive, para 42(2), names four mandatory categories, client accounts in a third person's name among them; remote customers are not among them.RahaPTS § 36(2), among its six cases: doubtful data, documents or beneficial owner; politically exposed persons (save § 38(5)); customers from, resident or seated in a high-risk third country; countries without effective AML systems or EU-listed as tax non-cooperative; corruption or sanctions links without the § 31(3) scheme.132150151152
Source trap

'Non-resident' hides three tests. LHV prices by the owners' residence and exempts Estonian citizens living abroad; Swedbank's EUR 50 and Coop Pank's EUR 500 turn on the country that issued an owner's identity document, or registered an owning company, Cyprus being named beside the non-EEA countries, and spare holders of Estonian documents (Coop: Estonian citizens and residence-permit holders); Bank of Cyprus's surcharge turns on where the entity is incorporated.9699103104

Source trap

Money at a payment or e-money institution is not a guaranteed deposit. In Cyprus it is safeguarded, not deposit-guaranteed (Law 31(I)/2018, art. 10(1)); in Estonia what such an institution keeps in a bank is outside the guarantee (Guarantee Fund Act § 30(1)). Directive (EU) 2026/804 has Member States guarantee such client funds, on conditions, from 11 May 2028.153154155

Source trap

The two shares of non-residents in bank deposits are not one count. Cyprus 11.9% at August 2026: non-MFI deposits with monetary financial institutions (MFIs) other than the CBC, in a CBC table where a company registered in Cyprus is a resident 'irrespective of whether or not' it has a physical presence there. Estonia 15.4% on 31 August 2026: deposits of all customer groups at banks, from Eesti Pank's table; its residence rule was not collected in this study. Both shares are computed in the records.156157158

Taxes: Cyprus taxes each year's income at 15%; Estonia, as a rule, taxes profit when paid out

Part 4, as of 4-5 October 2026. The two corporate taxes fall on different bases at different moments, and the payroll charges are built differently: a rate in one column is the counterpart of a rate in the other only where the first column says so. Each cell gives its country's rule as its own records state it.

The company: tax on profit, dividends and VAT

Corporate income tax (different bases, different moments)15% since 1 January 2026, 12.5% before (Law 244(I)/2025, art. 24(b)); provisional tax on the company's own estimate of the year's chargeable income, in two instalments, 31 July and 31 December in the Tax Department's 2026 calendar (Law 4/1978, art. 24).As a rule none on profit kept in the company; 22% of the amount divided by 0.78 ('22/78') when profit is paid out, since 1 January 2025; 20/80 from 2015 to 2024 (Income Tax Act §§ 4, 50). Relief and board fees under the Estonia–Cyprus convention (applied from 1 January 2014)Convention Art. 21(2): Estonian tax paid on income from Estonia is credited against Cyprus tax, 'subject to the provisions of Cyprus Tax Law regarding credit for foreign tax' (not collected in this study), up to the Cyprus tax on that income. Source trap

15% beside 22% compares two different taxes. The Cyprus rate applies to each year's chargeable income; the Estonian one, as a rule, only when profit is paid out, and also to fringe benefits, gifts and non-business expenses. On the dividend Cyprus then charges an individual owner who is tax resident there SDC, if also domiciled, and GeSY, with no domicile test; Estonia withholds nothing further, one transitional case aside.241242243244251252254

Source trap

8.8% plus 8.8% beside 33% is not like for like. The Social Tax Act splits Estonia's 33%, as a rule, into 20 points for state pension insurance and 13 for state health insurance, and sets no ceiling on an employee's salary. In Cyprus GeSY and the employer funds are separate charges, and social insurance stops at the ceiling of insurable earnings.253275277281283

Source trap

One company can meet both tests: Estonia's is establishment under its law alone, Cyprus's also control and management there. The convention leaves such a case to mutual agreement, 'due regard being had to the place of effective management ... or any other relevant criterion' (Art. 4(3)), untouched by the Multilateral Convention; failing that, Directive (EU) 2017/1852 offers an advisory commission. Per the Tax and Customs Board, a company managed from abroad 'will probably have a permanent establishment abroad'.257258259303304305306307

Source trap

Enacted, never applied. Estonia: the Security Tax Act, adopted 11 December 2024, was repealed from 18 July 2025 before its 2% tax on income and profit began; the rise of income tax to 24% (24/76 for companies) from 1 January 2026, enacted 18 June 2025, was struck out on 3 December 2025; the 24% VAT rate lost its planned 2029 return to 22% with that repeal. Cyprus: 17% on dividends to linked companies in low-tax jurisdictions, due under Law 48(I)/2025 from 1 January 2026, gave way that day to 5% (Law 245(I)/2025).262308309310311

Question 1 · Documents by stage

An OÜ is founded online in one application only if every signer holds an Estonian means of signing

As of 5 October 2026 a private limited company (osaühing, OÜ) has no minimum share capital. On an expedited-procedure application the state fee is EUR 265 and by law review is due no later than the next working day; otherwise the founding documents go through a notary. The annual report is due within six months of the end of the financial year.

  1. 1Means of signingbefore the application; for an e-resident's card the Police and Border Guard Board states a decision within 30 days of the application and then 2-5 weeks for the card to reach the chosen pick-up location, unless a mobile pick-up point was chosen (page read 5 October 2026)
  2. 2Nameno compulsory step; no record read sets its place in the order
  3. 3Capitalpaid in full before the application is filed (Commercial Code § 140(2))
  4. 4Address and contact personin the founding application; the e-Residency programme's page gives '1 day - 1 week' for getting a contact person
  5. 5Expedited applicationby law reviewed no later than the next working day after arrival (Commercial Register Act § 42(2)); 'usually takes one business day' per the e-Residency knowledge base (updated 15 December 2025)
  6. 6Notary routeby law reviewed within five working days of arrival (Commercial Register Act § 41(3)); the registrar may extend this up to three months for circumstances needing special verification (§ 41(5))
  7. 7Entrythe registrar notifies the applicant without delay (Commercial Register Act § 43(2))
  8. 8First dutieswork: no later than the moment it starts (Taxation Act § 25²); annual report: within six months of the end of the financial year (Commercial Code § 179(4))

The path of a new OÜ, as in force on 5 October 2026

  1. Means of signingbefore the application; for an e-resident's card the Police and Border Guard Board states a decision within 30 days of the application and then 2-5 weeks for the card to reach the chosen pick-up location, unless a mobile pick-up point was chosen (page read 5 October 2026)
    The founding application on the e-Business Register 'can be signed only with Estonian ID card (inc. e-residency card), Smart-ID and mobile-ID', and every person added to it - founders, board members, a contact person - 'must be able to digitally sign the application on the portal' (Centre of Registers and Information Systems, RIK, guide of 27 October 2025). The founder section covers the e-resident's card.5368
  2. Nameno compulsory step; no record read sets its place in the order
    No prior approval is compulsory: the portal's 'Name search', per RIK, 'does not give a definite answer', and the registrar decides during registration. Since 1 March 2024 a person may reserve one name for six months, for a stated legal form and field of activity (Commercial Code, Äriseadustik, § 15¹).10
  3. Capitalpaid in full before the application is filed (Commercial Code § 140(2))
    The law sets no minimum share capital; the smallest nominal value of a share is one cent (§ 148(1)). Where the cash contribution is not over EUR 50,000 the management board members confirm in the application that the contributions have been paid (§ 144(2¹)); a notice from a credit institution or payment institution is attached only if the contribution exceeds EUR 50,000 (§ 144(1) 3)).23314315
  4. Address and contact personin the founding application; the e-Residency programme's page gives '1 day - 1 week' for getting a contact person
    The seat is the place in Estonia determined by the articles of association, unless otherwise provided by law (General Part of the Civil Code Act § 29(1¹)). A legal person whose address is in a foreign state must appoint a contact person (Commercial Register Act, Äriregistri seadus, § 24), added in the same application per the portal guide; the entry needs that person's signed consent and a stated term (§ 24(5)-(6)).53667086316
  5. Expedited applicationby law reviewed no later than the next working day after arrival (Commercial Register Act § 42(2)); 'usually takes one business day' per the e-Residency knowledge base (updated 15 December 2025)
    Conditions of § 42(3), among others: the documents are digitally signed by the persons who must sign them; the standard articles of Commercial Code § 139¹ are used, to which the company cannot add provisions of its own; only cash contributions are made; the state fee has been received. Beneficial-owner details are filed together with the application (Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Prevention Act, the AML Act, §§ 76-77).5133485317
  6. Notary routeby law reviewed within five working days of arrival (Commercial Register Act § 41(3)); the registrar may extend this up to three months for circumstances needing special verification (§ 41(5))
    Outside the expedited procedure the memorandum of association and the articles are notarially authenticated and signed by all founders; a sole founder signs a notarially authenticated foundation resolution (Commercial Code § 138(4)-(5)). By an outline assembled from the records this covers, among other cases, non-standard articles, a contribution in kind, a founder whose data the registrar cannot check automatically (e.g. a foreign legal person) and a founder or board member unable to sign digitally.613318
  7. Entrythe registrar notifies the applicant without delay (Commercial Register Act § 43(2))
    The company receives a unique registry code (§ 22(1)) and, as notification, an uncertified printout of the valid entries of the register (§ 43(3)). The Act, as in force on 5 October 2026, provides for no certificate of incorporation: no words for one were found in §§ 22, 31 or 43. The company does not apply separately to be registered as a taxpayer - a reading of Taxation Act § 18(1), not a sentence of it.2022
  8. First dutieswork: no later than the moment it starts (Taxation Act § 25²); annual report: within six months of the end of the financial year (Commercial Code § 179(4))
    The person providing work registers its start in the Tax and Customs Board's employment register; so are members of a legal person's management or controlling body whose working gives rise to a tax liability in Estonia. VAT registration is by application; the founding application may carry a request for voluntary VAT registration and employee entries (RIK guide, 27 October 2025). The financial year is the calendar year unless the articles say otherwise (Accounting Act, Raamatupidamise seadus, § 13).2226293853319

Recurring duties of an OÜ, as in force on 5 October 2026

DutyRule and instrumentDeadline, sanction and dates
Annual reportThe management board files the approved report with the commercial register, with the proposal for distributing profit or covering loss, a breakdown of sales revenue by field of activity and, where audit is compulsory, the sworn auditor's report (Commercial Code § 179(4), (6)).Within six months of the end of the financial year. The registrar may, without first issuing a warning order, impose a fine repeatedly until the report is filed, weighing how often and how long it has been overdue (Commercial Register Act § 57(1)-(2)); such a fine may be up to EUR 3,200 (Code of Civil Procedure § 46(1)).3850
Missing annual report: deletionAfter the legal deadline the registrar sets a time limit for filing with a warning of deletion (Commercial Register Act § 61, in force since 1 February 2023).The company may be deleted if it has not filed within that limit and at least three months have passed since the statutory filing date; § 60(2), (5) adds further conditions. Restoration can be sought within three years (§ 64(2)).51
Beneficial-owner detailsChanges are filed through the commercial register information system; if nothing has changed, the company confirms the details when filing its annual report (AML Act §§ 76-77).Changes within 30 days of learning of them. A legal person is fined up to EUR 400,000, a shareholder or member up to 300 fine units - in both cases only if the act has put an obliged entity in a position where it cannot apply the due-diligence measure of § 20(1) p 3 (§ 95).34320
BookkeepingEvery legal person registered in Estonia is an accounting entity and must document and record all its transactions and keep its accounting documents (Accounting Act § 2(2), § 4). The management board organises the accounting (Commercial Code § 183).Source documents are kept for seven years from the end of the financial year (§ 12(1)).4647
Annual report: form and signingAnnual accounts and a management report; a micro-undertaking applying the Estonian financial reporting standard may prepare annual accounts only (Accounting Act § 14(1), (1¹)). Drawn up in Estonian (§ 25(1)); at least one member of the executive management signs it once approved (§ 25(3)).Since 17 January 2025, for periods from 1 January 2024: no e-signature where it is prepared, approved and filed through the e-Business Register with secure identification (§ 25(4)). The Act excepts a report subject to audit; the Centre of Registers' page (2 April 2025) one to be audited, reviewed or revised.321322

15 Jan 2018Until 14 January 2018 Commercial Code § 63¹(2) made an OÜ name a person with an Estonian address for service if at least half of its board members did not reside in Estonia, another state of the European Economic Area (EEA) or Switzerland.A 'contact person' was compulsory when the management board 'is located in a foreign state'; only listed professionals could be appointed, but a board member, shareholder or procurator residing in Estonia could also be (§ 63¹(3)).Commercial Code § 63¹ (RT I, 20.04.2017, 1)71

Open look again by 5 Jan 2027

Beneficial-owner data are public (AML Act § 78(1)). The Ministry of Finance said on 8 June 2026 that unauthenticated users would lose access from 10 July 2026, citing the EU's new anti-money-laundering framework and Court of Justice case law. Its release of 10 July 2026 says the planned changes are not applied, current access rules stay meanwhile and the date will be announced once the regulation is adopted. On 5 October 2026 a company page still showed the owner without login; no new date had been published.353682334

Not there look again by 5 Jan 2027

No statistic of the processing times the registrar achieves was found on 5 October 2026 - not on the page of the Tartu County Court registration department, the justdigi.ee commercial-register page or the register's statistics pages, whose charts are embedded dashboards; the e-Residency knowledge base's 'usually takes one business day' comes without figures. The fines imposed in practice for a late annual report were not established either.5085

Open look again by 5 Apr 2027

The law names where a cash contribution goes: a payment account in the company's name with an EEA credit or payment institution (Commercial Code §§ 141, 520(4)) or, in the expedited procedure, a deposit with the registrar or on the start-up account of AML Act § 27(2) (§ 520(4¹)); it names no e-money institution. By an outline from the records, nothing checks a contribution of up to EUR 50,000: the board only confirms payment. No source read on 5 October 2026 says what a founder does who cannot open such an account.105327335

Open look again by 5 Apr 2027

Why the minimum capital was removed in 2023 and the state fees were raised in 2022 is not established: for both changes the records give the texts of the acts and no statement of reasons.11330

Not established in this section

  • The registrar's processing times. How long does the registrar actually take to enter a company, in the expedited and in the ordinary procedure, as a published statistic? look again by 5 Jan 2027
  • Where the share capital sits before registration. Where does a founder who cannot open an account for the company being founded hold a cash contribution of up to EUR 50,000, and does an account at an e-money institution satisfy the Commercial Code? look again by 5 Apr 2027
  • Fines and deletions in practice. What fines does the registrar impose in practice for a late annual report, and how many private limited companies are entered and deleted each year? look again by 5 Jan 2027

Question 1, continued · A founder who does not live in Estonia

Founder abroad: no residence condition in the Code's OÜ part; only Estonian tools sign on the portal

The Commercial Code's private limited company (OÜ) part sets no residence or nationality condition for a founder or board member (5 October 2026). Signing: an Estonian eID such as the e-resident's digital ID (EUR 150, collected in person, closed to twelve states' citizens save exceptions) or a notary. Board fees are taxed in Estonia wherever members live.

By group: the route to a signature

FounderWhat the records stateInstrument
EveryoneThe Commercial Code's part on the private limited company (OÜ), as in force on 5 October 2026, sets no condition of Estonian residence or of nationality for a founder or board member.Commercial Code §§ 135–22015
Citizen of another EU stateLiving in Estonia: outside e-Residency, with an ID card issued on the basis of the right of residence, which the e-Business Register accepts for signing. Living elsewhere: no claim to that card; a home-state electronic identity (eID) logs in to the register but cannot sign.Police and Border Guard Board (Politsei- ja Piirivalveamet, PPA) page, read 5 October 2026; Centre of Registers' help page dated 12 November 2025336337
Any citizen of a foreign state, third-country nationals includedThe e-resident's digital ID is issued to a citizen of a foreign state who holds no valid Estonian identity card or residence permit card and is not applying for one at the same time. Issue may be refused when not consistent with the card's purpose; per the Tallinn Administrative Court (2023) the card is a benefit, not a right.Identity Documents Act § 20⁵(1), wording in force since 6 July 2025; § 20⁶(3) 2); Tallinn Administrative Court, 18 May 2023, case 3-22-85859338339

'Permanent economic activity in Estonia', as the PPA explains itThe applicant owns a company in Estonia or has a direct connection with one, for example as a management board member; the company is active, has sales revenue and/or staff and pays taxes to the Estonian state, with annual reports, tax returns and beneficial-ownership information duly filed.PPA page, read 5 October 2026340

Signing without the e-resident's ID

  • Login is not signing. The state authentication service TARA, offered to institutions for adding login methods to their e-services, listed Cyprus among 22 country scopes for cross-border login on 5 October 2026; this says nothing about signing. Art. 6 of Regulation (EU) No 910/2014 (eIDAS) covers authentication only; electronic signatures fall under its Arts 24a and 27.348349
  • In the e-Business Register, per the Centre of Registers' help page dated 12 November 2025, EU eID tools log in 'to view the data'; statements and reports are signed only with an Estonian ID card (including the e-residency card), Estonian Smart-ID or Estonian Mobile-ID. Its portal page (read 5 October 2026) makes electronic establishment conditional on all related persons being able to sign with one of those tools.337
  • Notary route. Without an Estonian eID the memorandum of association and the articles are notarially authenticated and signed by all founders; a representative signs only under a notarially certified power of attorney (Commercial Code § 138(4)).54
  • Signatures on a register application may also be certified by a foreign official entitled to verify the signatory's identity; a document certified abroad must be legalised or carry an apostille unless an international agreement provides otherwise (Commercial Register Act § 40(1)).350
  • Remote authentication by a notary over video link is at the notary's discretion, and participants sign the deed digitally (Notarisation Act § 13(7)). For it the Chamber of Notaries names only Estonian means of entering its portal and signing, the e-resident's digital ID among them (page dated 20 January 2023, read 5 October 2026).55
  • The Tax and Customs Board's portal e-MTA accepts login with other EEA states' high-assurance eIDs; its list (page read 5 October 2026) names 23 states, Cyprus among them ('Cyprus National eID', IDMe.cy). At first login an account is created and customer support then opens the e-services; access for a company is requested separately. Mobile-ID and Smart-ID need an Estonian personal identification code.351352
Source trap

Older pages describe a different portal. Archived pages of the Centre of Registers show signing with foreign identities on 5 January 2016, 18 August 2019 ('an Estonian, Latvian, Belgian or Finnish ID card or Estonian or Lithuanian Mobile ID') and 16 January 2021, and Estonian tools only on 2 April 2023. On 5 October 2026 the portal page says applications and reports 'can only be signed with an Estonian ID-card, Smart-ID, and Mobile-ID'. The date and instrument of the change were not found.8081

Contact person and address

  • A company whose address is in a foreign state must appoint a contact person in Estonia: only a notary, an advocate or law-firm operator, a sworn auditor or audit firm, a non-resident's tax representative or a licensed trust and company service provider (Commercial Register Act § 24(2), (4)). A company that fails to appoint one may be struck off after a warning and three further months (§ 62).353

Tax: the Estonian rule beside the Estonia–Cyprus convention

PointEstonian law or authorityConvention of 15 October 2012, in effect from 1 January 2014; EU law
Residence of the companyA legal person is resident 'if it is established under Estonian law'. The Act has no place-of-management test for legal persons; § 6(5) gives way to a tax treaty where residence under the treaty differs (Income Tax Act § 6(2), (5)).Art. 4(3): a company's dual residence is settled by mutual agreement, regarding the place of effective management or any other relevant criterion; no fallback. Directive (EU) 2017/1852 (in Estonia Taxation Act chapter 14¹, from 15 November 2019) adds, on request, an Advisory Commission whose opinion binds if they still disagree.303306356
Company managed from abroadEMTA, page updated 4 November 2025: an Estonian company that an e-resident manages from outside Estonia 'will probably have a permanent establishment abroad and income tax must be paid on the profit earned by this Estonian company in that foreign state'.Art. 5(2)(a) counts 'a place of management' among permanent establishments; Art. 7(1) lets the other state tax 'only so much of them as is attributable to that permanent establishment'. The convention does not say when a director working from home makes a place of management.307357
Profit of a permanent establishment abroadDividends paid out of profit attributed to an establishment in an EEA state or Switzerland are exempt from the Estonian distribution tax; for an establishment in any other state, only if that profit 'has been taxed' (Income Tax Act § 50(1¹) 2), 4)).Art. 21: Estonia exempts income of its resident which under the convention 'has been taxed in Cyprus'.300358
Dividend to an owner resident in CyprusA dividend is taxed once, at company level; nothing is withheld from the shareholder, resident or non-resident, save one transitional exception (Income Tax Act § 61(68)). As of 5 October 2026.Art. 10(1): 'taxable only in that other State'. Art. 10(3) excepts dividends effectively connected with a permanent establishment the recipient has in the paying company's state. The convention has no rate table for dividends.254313
Board member's feeTaxable in Estonia 'regardless of where the work was actually done', per EMTA (page updated 14 November 2025); the basis is Income Tax Act § 29(2). Income tax 22% (§ 4(1)), social tax 33% (Social Tax Act § 7(1)), both rates in force on 5 October 2026; no social tax with a home-country Form A1 (below).Art. 15: 'may be taxed' in the company's state, which does not make the taxation exclusive. Art. 21: Cyprus allows the Estonian tax as a credit, capped at the Cyprus tax on that income, 'subject to the provisions of Cyprus Tax Law regarding credit for foreign tax'.294300302359
Residence of the personPlace of residence in Estonia, or a stay of at least 183 days in a period of 12 consecutive calendar months (Income Tax Act § 6(1)). e-Residency is not among the tests.Art. 4(1): a resident is a person liable to tax by reason of domicile, residence, place of management, place of incorporation or a similar criterion. A treaty tie-breaker prevails over the statutory test (Income Tax Act § 6(5)).299303
Salary for work done outside EstoniaNot taxed in Estonia when a non-resident employee works outside Estonia, per EMTA (page updated 4 November 2025), which adds that a tax liability may arise abroad and asks that payments to a sole shareholder who is also director and worker be split into salary, board fee and dividends 'according to their actual substance'.Art. 14(1): salary of a resident of one state is taxable only there unless the employment is exercised in the other state. Art. 15 treats directors' fees separately. In force on 5 October 2026.301360

Board member's fee: social tax and the A1 certificate

  • Social tax (33%) is due on fees paid to a member of a management or controlling body (Social Tax Act § 2(1) 4), § 7(1)). The exemption for pay to a non-resident whose place of work is in a foreign state (§ 3 point 7) does not list such fees.294361
  • Form A1. Per EMTA (page updated 4 November 2025), social tax is not due 'only if the recipient of such remuneration has been issued in his or her home country a certificate of social security coverage valid in such foreign country on Form A1'.294
  • EMTA's social tax page (updated 20 July 2026), applying Regulation (EC) No 883/2004: 'If the A1 certificate has been issued in another Member State, there will be no social tax liability in Estonia and the employee's social security contributions must be paid according to the rules of that Member State.' In Estonia the Social Insurance Board decides the state of affiliation and issues A1.362
  • The programme's guidance (updated 19 January 2026) sorts three cases: a person living and insured in an EU state, Norway, Iceland, Liechtenstein or Switzerland should obtain an A1 from the home authority; a person insured in Canada, Ukraine or Australia may present the certificate under the social security agreement; residents of other third countries 'will not be able to get an exemption'.363
  • A board member living outside the EU, the EEA and Switzerland has no A1 under Regulation (EC) No 883/2004, so by the study's reading social tax of 33% is due on the fee unless a social security agreement says otherwise (as of 5 October 2026). EMTA lists agreements with Ukraine, Canada, Russia, Australia and Belarus; the Social Insurance Board's list also has Moldova.364
  • Social tax on a board fee gives Estonian health insurance only on two conditions: the member is a permanent resident of Estonia, lives there on a temporary residence permit or right of residence, or legally stays and works there temporarily (Health Insurance Act § 5(1)); and social tax is paid for him or her on at least the monthly rate, EUR 886 in 2026 per EMTA (§ 5(2) point 4, in force since 1 January 2016).365

Living in Estonia as a founder

RouteWho and on what termsInstrument and published time
Temporary residence permit for enterpriseNeeded by a third-country national to live in Estonia as a company owner, not by citizens of EU states, Iceland, Liechtenstein, Norway and Switzerland. A shareholder needs at least EUR 65,000 invested in the share capital of an Estonian company and spent on real estate, machinery or equipment booked as fixed assets in Estonia.Aliens Act § 192(2). PPA page, 5 October 2026: a reply within 90 days, a permit of up to 5 years. The shareholder route counts against the annual immigration quota (§ 113(2)); § 115 leaves out start-up and large-investor permits and citizens of, among others, the United States, Japan and the United Kingdom.366367
Start-upA start-up, as the Act defines it, is exempt from the EUR 65,000 investment and business-plan requirements of that permit; a short- or long-stay visa for start-up entrepreneurship also exists. Per the PPA, an expert committee of the Ministry of the Interior evaluates the company within 10 working days of a complete application.Aliens Act § 192(2¹) 2), (6¹), in force since 18 January 2017; § 62⁴368
Large investorAn alien who has made a direct investment of at least EUR 1,000,000 in a company in the Estonian commercial register that invests mainly in the Estonian economy, or in an investment fund investing mainly in such companies, kept for the permit's term. The conditions of actual residence and of registering a residence are lifted.Aliens Act § 197³(1)–(2), § 197⁴369
Visa for remote work ('digital nomad visa')For an alien whose work does not depend on location and who continues working for an employer registered abroad, doing business for a company registered abroad in which he or she has a holding, or serving clients mainly abroad. It gives a temporary stay, not a residence permit.Aliens Act § 62⁵, in force since 1 July 2020370
Restrictions: Russian and Belarusian citizensCovers every Russian citizen applying for any visa or a temporary residence permit for study, employment or enterprise, and every Belarusian citizen applying for a visa for short-term employment, start-up entrepreneurship, remote work or enterprise or a residence permit for employment or enterprise. Listed exceptions apply.Government Regulation No 42 of 8 April 2022, § 2(1)–(1²), in force since 21 May 2023; extending an employment or enterprise permit is outside it (§ 2(3)); § 2(6) excepts some visa cases. Separately, Order No 247 of 8 September 2022 restricts entry by Russian citizens with a valid short-stay visa. Both in force on 5 October 2026.371372373
Young company with state or licensed-fund backingThe EUR 65,000 does not apply where the company has been in the commercial register under 12 months and starts with an investment or loan from the state or a private fund manager licensed by Finantsinspektsioon (the financial supervisor), or a state support grant. It still needs the business-plan description of § 192(5).Aliens Act § 192(2¹) 1), wording in force since 1 January 2026 (RT I, 17.04.2025, 2); § 192(6¹) names start-ups only374

Why the 2025 stop was adopted, and what is proposed

  • Why. The Government's explanatory memorandum to bill 572 SE, which created the citizenship-based stop and was adopted on 4 June 2025, records negative decisions on first applications rising from 2% in 2018 to 8% in 2023 and cards revoked after follow-up checks numbering 29 in 2020 and 267 in 2023.79
  • The memorandum says background checks alone do not reduce risk enough for states with which Estonia has no judicial, security or law-enforcement cooperation, and that existing cards stay valid until expiry or revocation.79
  • Proposed. Bill 905 SE, initiated by the Government on 7 May 2026, stood at the second-reading stage in the Riigikogu (parliament) on 5 October 2026, not adopted. Per the Riigikogu's summary, e-residents' identity would be verified remotely by biometrics from March 2027, and from March 2028 a card-free 'e-identification means' would replace the digital ID card.88
  • The bill's text tabled for the first reading leaves the EUR 150 state fee as it is.375

e-resident cards revoked after follow-up checks, per the explanatory memorandum to bill 572 SE

Not there look again by 5 Jan 2027

No enacted change was found on 5 October 2026 for the e-Residency state fee or for the founder-migration rules: the versions of the State Fees Act already published for 1 November 2026, 1 January 2027 and 1 July 2027 keep EUR 150, and the version of the Aliens Act published for 1 January 2027 leaves §§ 62⁴, 62⁵, 192 and 197³ word for word the same. An Act adopted after 11 July 2026 and not yet consolidated would not show in the State Fees Act versions.376377378

Not established: how the Social Insurance Board (Sotsiaalkindlustusamet) and EMTA classify a board member's mandate under Regulation (EC) No 883/2004, which has no rule specific to company directors, and what the company must do in the member's state of residence. Also: whether the registrar or a notary accepts in practice a signature certified in Cyprus with an apostille; whether the register's signing step takes a foreign qualified electronic signature (not tested).75350379380

Open look again by 5 Jan 2027

Open on 5 October 2026: whether bill 905 SE is adopted and with which dates; and which amount the PPA applies to the remote-work visa. The programme page states 'EUR 4,500 net/monthly'. Minister of the Interior Regulation No 82 of 18 December 2015, § 15(7), sets 0.60 times the subsistence level per day of stay since 1 January 2026 (0.75 until 31 December 2025) and does not say net or gross. By the study's arithmetic that is EUR 3,960 for 30 days in 2026; EUR 4,500 is the 2025 result.88370381

Question 2 · Agreements with the bank and with payment providers

Swedbank, SEB and LHV sign the merchant contract themselves; Coop Pank works through partners

On 5 October 2026 Swedbank, SEB and LHV are themselves the contracting party for terminals; Coop Pank works with Nets and Montonio. Stripe, Adyen, PayPal, Wise, myPOS and Viva contract under another state's law; SumUp's Estonian terms choose Estonian law. Montonio's merchant terms were not among its published documents; Revolut's live pages were not read.

Estonian payment law draws its line at the consumer, and the Law of Obligations Act (Võlaõigusseadus) has no word for micro-enterprise: Estonia did not use the option of the second Payment Services Directive (PSD2) to treat micro-enterprises as consumers. Under § 733^14(6), wording in force since 11 March 2023, where the client 'is not a consumer' the parties may agree that listed provisions do not apply in part or in full - among them § 719^1 (two months' notice of changes), § 720 (cancellation) and § 733^6 (refunds) - and may agree a period other than the 13 months of § 733^7(2) for reporting unauthorised payments.383

Dated versions: how the terms changed

Question 3 · Entry points and the market in numbers

Swedbank, SEB and LHV sign merchants themselves; Coop Pank works through Nets and Montonio

Swedbank, SEB and LHV are the acquiring banks at the terminal and behind the EveryPay gateway online; Coop Pank refers merchants to Nets and Montonio; Luminor is the acquirer named in Maksekeskus's terms. On 5 October 2026 the supervisor's register also lists eleven Estonian payment and four e-money institutions, and several hundred passported entries.

On 5 October 2026 the register of Finantsinspektsioon, the financial supervisor, lists eight licensed credit institutions and five branches of foreign banks. Its market overview puts companies' deposits at the end of 2025 at Swedbank 37%, LHV Pank 27%, SEB Pank 23%, Coop Pank 6% and Luminor Bank 3%.440441

Bank payments and instant payments beside cards

  • In Eesti Pank's statistics of payments sent by households, summed by year, 'bank link payment orders' peaked at 29.8 million in 2021 and fell to 14.5 million in 2025; 'payment initiated with another service provider', a row new in 2022 with 13.1 million, reached 28.9 million in 2025, beside 492.2 million household card payments.175
  • Eight Estonian providers stood in the European Payments Council's register of instant credit transfer on 5 October 2026: SEB (ready 21 November 2017), Swedbank, LHV, Coop Pank, Luminor, Bigbank and the e-money institutions inHouse Pay AS and Monemon AS.174
  • Eesti Pank's Payments Review of March 2026 puts instant payments at 87% of the monthly number of domestic payments in December 2025, a share it charts among interbank transfers, not card payments.460
  • Under Article 5a of Regulation (EU) No 260/2012 as amended by Regulation (EU) 2024/886, providers in the euro area that offer their users ordinary credit transfers had to receive instant credit transfers in euro by 9 January 2025 and send them by 9 October 2025; payment institutions and e-money institutions located in the euro area must offer both by 9 April 2027.461
  • On direct debit, Eesti Pank's SEPA page (read 5 October 2026) says 'The banks operating in Estonia do not offer direct debit services'. Its FAQ, written in early 2014, is narrower: banks do not let companies collect by SEPA direct debit within Estonia, while a bill abroad can be paid by cross-border SEPA direct debit from an Estonian account.462
