Question 4 · An online business and an offline business

Two outlines: an online shop whose board member lives abroad, and a café in Tallinn

Both walk-throughs are outlines assembled from the records, with prices as of 5 October 2026. In the portal the shop's founding application can be signed only with an Estonian ID-card (the e-resident's card included), Mobile-ID or Smart-ID. An ordinary café notifies instead of obtaining a food licence; a cash register is required once it sells alcohol.

Both blocks are outlines assembled from the records; their stage order is their own, and records give a reason for it at a few points only. In the portal the founding application can be signed only with an Estonian ID card (the e-resident's card included), Smart-ID or Mobile-ID; a founder without one first obtains one or goes through a notary. Entries filed with the application reach the Tax and Customs Board once the company is entered. Stripe asks for an IBAN for payouts; LHV's terminal terms require an LHV account. A notified food business 'may operate after notifying'; an employee is registered no later than the moment work starts. Elsewhere the documents read set no order.54164319471472473474

Outline (i): an online shop run by a private limited company (OÜ) whose only board member lives in another EU state

  1. A means of signaturePolice and Border Guard Board: decision within 30 days; the card reaches the pick-up location 2-5 weeks later
    Outline assembled from the records. In the e-Business Register portal one signs only with an Estonian ID-card (the e-resident's card included), Mobile-ID or Smart-ID; other EU eIDs only allow viewing data. One route without these is the e-resident's digital ID: state fee EUR 150 (State Fees Act § 272(6)), not refunded on refusal (the Board) or when the application is left without review (§ 15^1); collected in person. Applications from twelve listed states' citizens are reviewed only under listed exceptions.471475476477478
  2. Founding application
    Outline assembled from the records. In the portal he enters the principal activity, name, address - a foreign address needs a contact person - e-mail, board members and founders, capital (from EUR 0.01 with one shareholder), articles and beneficial owners, signs digitally and pays the state fee: EUR 265 on an expedited-procedure application, EUR 200 otherwise (State Fees Act § 62(2)). 'As a rule, it is not necessary to add mandatory documents.' A request for voluntary VAT registration may be added.319471475478
  3. Settlement account at an Estonian bankSwedbank's checklist: decision within 10 working days after the fee is paid, if all documents are submitted; SEB: 'may take up to 10 working days'
    Outline assembled from the records, as of 5 October 2026. Fees: Swedbank EUR 300 for enhanced due diligence where non-residents represent or own the company; SEB EUR 500 to handle documents in that case (EUR 0 until 30 November 2026 per its offers page; its Estonian text excludes non-EEA residents); LHV EUR 300 to apply and EUR 30 a month where non-residents hold at least 25% or the beneficiary is non-resident (not for Estonian citizens abroad). None is refunded on refusal. All three weigh the link to Estonia.102120479480481482483484
  4. An account abroad as the alternative
    Outline assembled from the records, as of 5 October 2026. Or an account abroad: Wise Europe SA (Belgian law; account details a one-off EUR 50) or Paysera LT UAB (EUR 5 a month for a SEPA-registered company, or EUR 100 and EUR 20 a month if it is an 'international company'). Stripe's EUR payouts for Estonia reach banks in 32 countries, Belgium, Lithuania and Cyprus among them; it accepts 'virtual bank accounts (such as N26, Revolut, and Wise)': 'you might see higher payout failures for these accounts'.229472479485
  5. Card provider: who is accepted, which contract
    Outline assembled from the records. Stripe accepts companies registered in Estonia through e-Residency if the people associated with the account live in a Stripe-supported country (Estonia and Cyprus are listed); from a person living in a country other than the account's it takes only a valid passport. It contracts through its Irish entities under Irish law. It compares ownership data with the Centre of Registers; payouts 'will be paused' if the business cannot be found there and no confirmation is submitted.208486487488
  6. Tariff and first payoutStripe's documentation for Estonia: initial settlement 7 calendar days, default 3 business days from capture; risk criteria may keep an account off the default
    Outline assembled from the records, as of 5 October 2026. Stripe: 1.5% + EUR 0.25 for standard EEA cards. Local checkouts add bank payments: Montonio (cards through Adyen; Core EUR 14.99 a month, 1.29% + EUR 0.20 for EU consumer cards, without VAT; payout T+2 daily) and Maksekeskus (Luminor as acquirer; 1.35% + EUR 0.15 plus EUR 10 a month, VAT added; card payouts 'with a 7-day delay'). PayPal: 3.4% + EUR 0.35 per commercial transaction, 1.9% + fixed fee for a card payment by a payer without a PayPal account.203204209225236403405406486
  7. VATregistration compulsory on the day turnover exceeds EUR 40,000 from the start of the calendar year
    Outline assembled from the records. The threshold counts turnover with Estonia as place of supply (Value-Added Tax Act § 19(1)). Goods sent to consumers in other EU states keep Estonia as place of supply only while the seller is established in no other member state, buyers are not VAT-registered and its distance sales plus electronically supplied services exceeded EUR 10,000 neither last nor this calendar year (§ 10^1, since July 2021); the seller may opt earlier for the customer's state, for two years at least.489490491
  8. Income tax and the board member's feedeclared on form TSD by the 10th day of the month after payment, per the Tax and Customs Board
    Outline assembled from the records. Income tax falls on distributed profit upon payment: 22%, the taxable amount divided by 0.78. The board member's fee is taxed in Estonia although he lives abroad: income tax withheld at 22% unless a tax treaty sets a lower rate, and social tax 33%, not due, per the Tax and Customs Board, only with an A1 certificate from his home country. Per its page, a company an e-resident manages from abroad 'will probably have a permanent establishment abroad', with that profit taxed there.249489490492493
Source trap

The Tax and Customs Board's page on e-residents' companies, read 5 October 2026, still says a board member's fee is declared in Annex 2 of form TSD. Minister of Finance Regulation No 21 of 30 September 2026 (RT I, 02.10.2026, 6) repealed that annex: Riigi Teataja shows it in force from 5 October 2026, its own § 2 names 1 October. Payments to residents and non-residents now go into one part of form TSD, 'Väljamaksed', with fields for the state that issued the A1 certificate and the state of work.493494495496497

Outline (ii): a café in Tallinn run by an OÜ with a resident owner

  1. Company
    Outline assembled from the records. The owner signs the founding application in the e-Business Register portal with an Estonian ID-card, Mobile-ID or Smart-ID; state fee EUR 265; capital from EUR 0.01 with one shareholder. Entries of employees can go in the same application and are forwarded to the Tax and Customs Board once the company is entered.319471498
  2. Bank accountSwedbank's page promises contact within an hour for an application sent on a working day between 8:30 and 16:00
    Outline assembled from the records, as of 5 October 2026. Swedbank: contracts signed in its Internet bank with Smart-ID, Mobile-ID or ID card; first year EUR 0 for the account fee, euro payments and debit card, cards free 'for start-up companies'. SEB: plans EUR 1-15 a month, one free for a year for a new business customer's first account ('by 30 November 2026' in the offer block; no end date in the footnote). LHV: company account opened and kept free, 'except non-residents and business entities related to them'.480481483498499
  3. Food noticebefore operating; no state fee
    Outline assembled from the records. An ordinary café needs no activity licence: under Food Act § 7(1) it notifies the Agriculture and Food Board (Põllumajandus- ja Toiduamet), whose page says a notified business 'may operate after notifying' and a written self-check plan need not be filed but must exist and be available. Supervision afterwards is charged by the hour (Food Act § 49^3): EUR 40.84 in 2026, at most eight hours per act, payable within 28 days of receiving the payment decision.473500501502
  4. Alcohol notice and cash register
    Outline assembled from the records. Serving alcohol takes a second notice of economic activities, for catering (Alcohol Act § 23(1)), filed only through eesti.ee or a notary. Retail sale of alcoholic drinks in a catering establishment is permitted only if a cash register at the place of business records every such sale (§ 44); not recording one is a misdemeanour, for a legal person a fine of up to EUR 3,000 (§ 62). In Tallinn the city supervises retail sale and its council may restrict it.500503504505
  5. Staffno later than the moment the person starts work
    Outline assembled from the records. The employer enters each employee's start of work in the employment register and registers suspension and termination within ten days (Taxation Act § 25^2). The register also covers members of a legal person's management body where their work gives rise to a tax liability in Estonia.474500
  6. Terminal from a bank or Nets
    Outline assembled from the records, as of 5 October 2026. Swedbank: commission 'as agreed'; printed tariffs: phone terminal 0.99% + EUR 0.02, smart terminal 1.45%; rent EUR 13-45 a month plus VAT (3-month minimum). SEB and LHV: fee by contract; LHV rent EUR 12.50-20 a month plus VAT, money 'the next day, 7 days a week'. Coop Pank: through Nets, quote 'depends on the volume of card payments'; a company under 6 months old gets up to 3 terminals rent-free for 3 months, repayable if the contract ends within a year.400401429430439506
  7. Terminal from a provider with a published tariffSumUp: identity check before payments 'may take several days'; payouts 'by the next business day' (price page), to a bank in 2-3 (help centre) or up to 3-5 business days (terms)
    Outline assembled from the records, as of 5 October 2026. Maksekeskus: 0.95% + EUR 0.02, rent EUR 13, VAT added; paid next business day. SumUp: 1.69%, or EUR 19 a month and 0.89% (1.69% on foreign, business, premium cards). myPOS: 1.45% + EUR 0.05 for EEA consumer cards, paid at once into its own account. Viva: 1.69% for EEA consumer cards plus a monthly plan. Stripe Terminal: preview. No terminal: Wise, Montonio, Paysera, and PayPal on one reading.204210213215219230236237403411412413418419506507
  8. Receiptswhen goods or a service are paid for at once
    Outline assembled from the records. Consumer Protection Act § 4(4), worded from 1 September 2025, makes the trader give the consumer a proof of purchase: on paper on demand, and by default when the consumer pays cash without a customer card giving electronic access. Until 31 August 2025, below EUR 20 it was given on request (§ 4(5), repealed). A VAT invoice need not be issued to a natural person for personal use (three cases excepted). The VAT, Income Tax and Consumer Protection Acts do not mention a cash register.505508509
  9. VAT and income tax
    Outline assembled from the records. Restaurant and catering services carry the standard VAT rate, 24% since 1 July 2025 (Value-Added Tax Act § 15(1)). Registration is compulsory on the day turnover with Estonia as place of supply exceeds EUR 40,000 from the start of the calendar year (§ 19(1)). Input VAT on receiving guests or on catering the company's own employees is not deductible (§ 30(1)). Income tax falls on distributed profit upon payment: 22%, the taxable amount divided by 0.78 (Income Tax Act §§ 4, 50).263490508
  10. Cash at the till
    A café must accept euro cash (up to 50 coins at a time, banknotes without limit) 'unless there is an agreement to use another means of payment' (Act on the Introduction of the Euro and the Settlement of Cash Payments in Euros § 2(2), since 1 March 2016); refusal is a misdemeanour (Consumer Protection Act § 74). No duty to accept cards was found there or in the Law of Obligations Act's payment chapters. Since 1 January 2025 cash at physical points of sale is rounded to the nearest 5 cents (§ 5-1); cards are not.186510
Source trap

The e-resident's digital ID serves only to identify and sign. LHV's page says it 'is not an identity document' and that 'Non-resident clients can not be digitally identified', so it asks for an office visit unless the person was already identified at LHV; SEB's page says an e-resident 'must visit a branch'. Swedbank advises an appointment at its Arter branch where the owners include non-residents or foreign companies; its checklist (26 March 2026) says such an account is not opened directly at the branch.120476482483

Source trap

Swedbank (section effective 27 August 2026): EUR 50 a month for a legal person needing regular enhanced due diligence, including one whose structure has a company registered in Cyprus, Liechtenstein or outside the EEA, or whose (beneficial) owners include a holder of an ID document issued there 'and who is not a resident of Estonia' (per the note: no Estonian-issued document). LHV's list (1 May 2024) keeps Cyprus out of its EU rate; on that wording a Cyprus-registered entity pays EUR 600 to apply, EUR 60 a month.511512

Open look again by 5 Apr 2027

Not established for the café: whether any Estonian act outside those read obliges a café to use a cash register or certified till, and which Tallinn city permits it needs (terrace, signage, opening hours, local alcohol restrictions). Read: the Value-Added Tax, Taxation, Income Tax, Consumer Protection, Food and Alcohol Acts. Not searched: the Accounting Act, the Trading Act, Tax and Customs Board guidance, Tallinn city regulations.503505

Open look again by 5 Jan 2027

Not established for the shop: whether Montonio or Maksekeskus accepts a company whose only representative lives abroad; which documents PayPal asks of an Estonian company; what the 'Preview' status of Stripe Terminal requires of a merchant. Not established for the café: how a resident-owned company opens an LHV account (channel, time). The providers' application forms or written answers would close the first three.210403411481

Not established in this section

  • A café's local permits and its till. Which Tallinn city permits does a café need (terrace, signage, opening hours, local alcohol restrictions), and does any act outside those read oblige it to use a cash register? look again by 5 Apr 2027

What is known on each point, and where it was looked for, is in the register at the end.

Question 5 · Laws, rules and supervisors

Finantsinspektsioon and the Financial Intelligence Unit over providers; consumer law over merchants

Finantsinspektsioon licenses payment and e-money institutions and supervises banks and financial institutions under it; the Financial Intelligence Unit is the default anti-money-laundering supervisor. Merchant rules - no surcharge on consumer cards, cash accepted and rounded, a purchase document - sit in the obligations, euro and consumer protection acts.

The acts and who supervises them (as of 5 October 2026)

ActWhat it carriesSupervisor or enforcing bodyStatus
Rahapesu ja terrorismi rahastamise tõkestamise seadus (RahaPTS) - Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Prevention ActDuties of obliged entities (§ 2), among them credit and financial institutions, traders in cash deals from EUR 10,000 and providers of trust and company services. A company-formation or virtual-office provider needs an authorisation from the Financial Intelligence Unit (§ 70(1) 2), § 71).§ 64: the Financial Intelligence Unit (Rahapesu Andmebüroo) by default; Finantsinspektsioon for the credit and financial institutions under its financial supervision; the Bar Association's board for attorneys; the Ministry of Justice and Digital Affairs for notaries.In force from 27 November 2017; consolidated text RT I, 11.07.2026, 93 in force from 21 July 2026, its header listing 20 amending acts; a version is published for 31.03.2029.127128130513
International Sanctions Act§ 21: a 'person with special obligations' checks, when a financial sanction starts, changes or ends, whether a present or prospective customer is sanctioned; omitting it at onboarding costs a legal person up to EUR 400,000 (§ 35^3).The Financial Intelligence Unit for 'persons with special obligations' (§ 30(1)); Finantsinspektsioon for the credit and financial institutions under its supervision (§ 30(1^1), added 15 March 2022).In force 1 January 2020; text in force 1 July 2026.514515
Makseasutuste ja e-raha asutuste seadus - Payment Institutions and E-money Institutions ActThe activity licence of a payment or e-money institution (§ 14). § 113-3 punishes a breach of Articles 3-5, 8, 10 and 12 of Regulation (EU) 2015/751 (interchange fees) with a fine of up to EUR 5,000,000 or up to twice the gain made or loss avoided, for a legal person alternatively up to 10% of its or its group's turnover.Finantsinspektsioon grants and revokes the licence (§ 14(1), (3)) and conducts the misdemeanour proceedings (§ 117(2)).In force 22 January 2010; § 113-3 so worded from 1 November 2023; consolidated text in force 01.10.2026-30.03.2029.516517
Law of Obligations Act (Võlaõigusseadus)The basic payment account (§ 710^1) and the surcharge rules (§ 725(7)-(9)). Where the client 'is not a consumer' the parties may agree that listed protections do not apply (§ 733^14(6)); the act has no word for micro-enterprise.The Consumer Protection and Technical Regulatory Authority may issue a precept against a trader breaching § 28-1 or § 725(9) (Consumer Protection Act § 65(2)).Consolidated text in force 1 September-31 December 2026.108179383518
Consumer Protection Act (Tarbijakaitseseadus); Act on the Introduction of the Euro and the Settlement of Cash Payments in Euros (the euro act)The purchase document, price indication and the misdemeanour for refusing cash; cash acceptance and rounding.The Consumer Protection and Technical Regulatory Authority: the surcharge ban, cash acceptance, rounding, receipts and price display sit with it.Consumer Protection Act: text in force from 27.09.2026. Euro act: text in force from 01.01.2025.186510517519520
Guarantee Fund ActEligible deposits are compensated with accrued interest up to EUR 100,000 per depositor in any one credit institution (§ 25(2)); a company's deposit is covered unless the company is an excluded entity of § 30. Deposits of an e-money institution or payment institution 'shall not be guaranteed or compensated' (§ 30(1)).The Guarantee Fund (Tagatisfond); it lists eight credit institutions (page last edited 04/06/2025). Per its FAQ, deposits in the Estonian branch of another EEA state's credit institution fall under the home state's scheme.Text in force from 21 July 2026.111154521

The Financial Intelligence Unit can act without a court order (§ 57 of RahaPTS, periods unchanged since 27 November 2017): on suspicion of money laundering or terrorist financing it may stop a transaction or restrict the disposal of an account or other property for up to 30 calendar days, and for a further 60 where the owner fails to prove the legal origin of the property within the first 30 days or terrorist financing is suspected. Its information requests reach data under banking secrecy (§ 58(2)). An obliged entity reports a suspicion without delay, within two working days at most (§ 49(1)).522

The rules a merchant meets

RuleWhat the act saysSection and actEnforcement and status
Surcharge on consumer cards and SEPA transfersA payee may not charge the payer a fee for using a payment instrument where interchange fees are regulated by Chapter II of Regulation (EU) 2015/751, nor for payment services covered by Regulation (EU) No 260/2012 (SEPA credit transfers and direct debits).Law of Obligations Act § 725(9)In force since 13 January 2018. The Consumer Protection and Technical Regulatory Authority may issue a precept.179518
Fee on other instrumentsOutside § 725(9) - in practice commercial cards and three-party schemes - the contract between the payee and its payment service provider 'may provide for' the right to agree a fee with the payer or offer a discount. A payer not told of the fee before initiating the payment owes nothing.Law of Obligations Act § 725(7)-(8)Since 15 July 2025 the fee is capped at the payee's actual cost of using the instrument (RT I, 08.07.2025, 4).180
Fee charged to a consumerA fee a trader charges a consumer for paying a contractual money obligation with a particular means of payment may not exceed the cost the trader incurs for the use of that means, whatever the payment instrument.Law of Obligations Act § 28-1(2)Precept by the same Authority (Consumer Protection Act § 65(2)).518
Accepting cash; accepting cardsPersons other than Eesti Pank and credit institutions must accept up to 50 valid euro coins at a time and banknotes without limit, 'unless there is an agreement to use another means of payment'. No provision requiring a merchant to accept cards or other electronic payments was found in the acts searched (see below).Euro act § 2(2), wording in force since 1 March 2016Refusal is a misdemeanour under § 74 of the Consumer Protection Act: up to 200 fine units, or up to EUR 40,000 for a legal person.186
Rounding of cash totalsCash payments at physical points of sale are rounded to the nearest five cents; card and other non-cash payments are not rounded, nor is a final price below three cents.Euro act § 5-1Since 1 January 2025; binds every trader; supervised by the same Authority (§§ 5-3 to 5-5).510
Cash thresholdsNo general ceiling on cash payments as of 5 October 2026; sector acts require non-cash settlement in the import, wholesale and export of alcohol and the wholesale of tobacco products (not retail). A trader paid, or paying, at least EUR 10,000 in cash - in one payment or linked payments within a year - becomes an obliged entity.RahaPTS § 2(1) point 5, § 19(2), § 49(3); Alcohol Act § 20(1); Tobacco Act § 20An obliged entity other than a credit institution reports to the Financial Intelligence Unit within two working days any transaction in which more than EUR 32,000 is paid in cash.188523
Cash ceiling fixed for 2027Persons trading in goods or providing services may accept or make a cash payment 'only up to an amount of EUR 10 000', in one operation or several linked ones. Not covered: payments between natural persons not acting professionally, and payments and deposits at credit institutions, e-money issuers and payment service providers.Regulation (EU) 2024/1624, Article 80Applies from 10 July 2027 (Article 90). Member States may set lower limits after consulting the European Central Bank; none was found in Estonian law on 5 October 2026.137
Purchase documentA trader must give the consumer a document proving the purchase when goods or services are paid for immediately: at least the trader's name and address, the date, each item's name and price and the amount paid. Paper on request, and by default for a cash purchase without a loyalty card giving electronic access.Consumer Protection Act § 4(4)-(4-2), (5-1)Wording in force from 1 September 2025. No paper where the consumer expressly agreed to receive the document by e-mail or another channel (§ 4(5-1)). The rule prescribes no device.519
Cash registerNo general duty to install a cash register was found in the Taxation Act on 5 October 2026; § 70(1) 4 and (2) let the tax authority demand one where a duty imposed by law was not met. Retail sale of alcohol in a shop or catering establishment is permitted only with a cash register recording every retail sale of alcohol.Taxation Act § 70; Alcohol Act § 44 (in force since 15 April 2004)Not recording such a sale: up to 200 fine units, or up to EUR 3,000 for a legal person (§ 62). The act does not describe the device or require a fiscal memory.524525
Price displayGoods: the selling price - the final price the consumer pays - and the unit price (unless the regulation under § 7(8) provides otherwise), in writing, clearly legible, unambiguous and easily noticeable. Services: the final price or, where it cannot be fixed in advance, its components and basis of calculation, and a price list.Consumer Protection Act §§ 7-8A price-reduction notice must show the earlier price (§ 7(6-1), since 28 May 2022).520

What the legislative history says of the surcharge ban: the Government's explanatory memorandum to bill 498 SE - the 2017 act transposing the second Payment Services Directive - states that the ban does not extend to payment cards whose fees are not regulated in Chapter II of Regulation (EU) 2015/751: 'such payment instruments are commercial cards'. Per the same memorandum, the Estonian Banking Association said terminal contracts already prohibited surcharges, and the Estonian Chamber of Commerce and Industry assessed the economic effect on traders as minimal 'since surcharging is not customary in Estonia'.235526

Question 6 · Taxes

Profit is taxed when paid out, at 22/78; pay bears 33% social tax; standard VAT is 24%

As a rule an Estonian company pays no income tax on profit it keeps: tax falls on distributions, declared monthly. Nothing is withheld from a dividend except a 7% transitional remnant. Two income tax rises enacted for 2026 were cancelled before they applied, and no income tax rate change is enacted for 2027 as of 5 October 2026.

The company: taxes and returns as of 5 October 2026

PointRuleInstrument
Income tax on distributed profit22% of the distributed amount divided by 0.78 (the Tax and Customs Board writes 22/78), paid by a resident company when it pays a dividend or other profit distribution. As a rule profit kept in the company bears no income tax; a resident credit institution, which pays quarterly advance tax on profit, is the exception.Income Tax Act § 4(1), (1^1), § 50(1), § 47^1; rate in force since 1 January 2025. The same tax falls on fringe benefits (§ 48), gifts, donations and entertainment expenses (§ 49), equity payouts above contributions (§ 50(2)), the transfer-pricing difference (§ 50(4)) and non-business expenses (§§ 51-52).243244
Monthly return, form TSDOne declaration of income and social tax, unemployment insurance premiums and funded pension contributions: a resident legal person files it on a calendar month's taxable payments and expenses by the 10th of the next month and pays by the same day. Per the Board dividends go into Annex 7, their recipients into form INF 1.Income Tax Act § 54(1), (2), (4); Minister of Finance Regulation No 60 of 29 November 2010. Per the Board there is no corporate tax return for the calendar year; a month with no taxable payments and no information to submit needs no return, but one is still due without tax where, for example, a contribution to equity is paid in.249250
Value-added tax (VAT) ratesStandard 24%; 13% on accommodation or accommodation with breakfast; 9% on books and educational literature, on listed medicines, hygiene products and disability devices, and on press publications not mainly carrying advertising or other listed content; 0% on exports and intra-Community supplies of goods, among others.Value-Added Tax Act § 15, in force 5 October 2026; the medical and hygiene goods are those listed by ministerial regulation. Restaurant and catering services bear the standard rate: § 15(1) covers everything the lists of § 15(1^1)-(4) do not, and these do not mention them.261263530
VAT on payment servicesExempt: payment services within the meaning of the Payment Institutions and E-money Institutions Act, and the issue and administration of non-cash means of payment such as electronic payment instruments and e-money. The Value-Added Tax Act does not name merchant acquiring as such.Value-Added Tax Act § 16(2^1) points 4 and 5. A supplier that has notified the Board in writing beforehand may add VAT to these services, except when supplying a taxable person of another member state, and is then bound for at least two years (§ 16(3) point 3, (4)).271
VAT registrationCompulsory from the day turnover with place of supply in Estonia exceeds EUR 40,000 in a calendar year; application within three working days. Voluntary before that. Either way the applicant must prove that it carries on or is starting business in Estonia, or the Board refuses.Value-Added Tax Act § 19(1), § 20(1), (2), (4^1). No duty arises if the year's turnover is all exempt and zero-rated supply (other than intra-Community supply of goods). The Board registers within five working days of the application (§ 20(3)).29531532
VAT returnsForm KMD for each calendar month, filed and paid by the 20th of the following month, with annex KMD INF (invoices with one counterparty totalling at least EUR 1,000 without VAT in the period, counted separately for sales and purchases) and report VD (intra-Community supplies).Value-Added Tax Act § 27(1), § 28, § 38(1). On reasoned application the tax authority may set a tax period longer than a month (§ 27(4)); bill 992 SE, not adopted, would end that. Per the Board a VAT-registered company files even with no supply in the period.250266267
Gifts and entertainment: limitsTaxed at 22/78 except advertising goods or services worth up to EUR 21 without VAT (EUR 10 until 31 December 2024), and guest and business-partner catering, accommodation, transport or entertainment up to EUR 50 a calendar month plus 2% of that month's payments subject to social tax (EUR 32 until 31 December 2024).Income Tax Act §§ 49, 51, in force 5 October 2026; limits raised by the act published RT I, 20.12.2024, 2. A payment lacking a source document that meets accounting rules counts as an expense unrelated to business.533
Selling to consumers in other member statesA seller established only in Estonia may keep Estonian VAT on distance sales of goods and of telecom and electronic services to consumers in other member states while these stay within EUR 10,000 in the previous and current year; otherwise it may use the one-stop-shop (OSS) scheme via e-MTA, quarterly.Value-Added Tax Act § 10^1(7)-(9), as of 5 October 2026; OSS return and payment by the end of the next month. Since 1 January 2025 a business with Union turnover up to EUR 100,000 may, after notifying the Estonian tax authority, use another member state's small-enterprise exemption within that state's threshold (§ 19^1).491534
Payment data sent by providers (CESOP)A payment service provider providing payment services for more than 25 cross-border payments to one payee in a quarter sends the Board data on the payee and the payments (name, VAT or other identifier, IBAN, date, amount, refunds and others) by the end of the following month. The duty lies on the provider, not on the merchant.Value-Added Tax Act § 36^1, in force since 1 January 2024 (act published RT I, 21.11.2023, 1); the provider keeps the records electronically for three calendar years.535
Late paymentInterest of 0.06% a day on tax paid late, from the day after the statutory due date up to and including the day of payment or set-off. When it grants payment by instalments the tax authority may cut the rate by up to 50% from its decision and, in a justified exceptional case, from the day the debt arose.Taxation Act § 115(1), § 117(1)-(2), in force 5 October 2026.536

The owner: dividends as of 5 October 2026

PointRuleInstrument
DividendTaxed once, at company level; nothing is withheld from the shareholder - resident or non-resident, natural or legal - with one transitional exception, the 7% in the next row.Income Tax Act § 18(1) taxes a resident natural person only on dividends from a foreign legal person; § 29 lists no dividend among a non-resident's Estonian-source income. Per the Board, from 2025 dividends 'are only taxed at the company level in Estonia with income tax at the rate of 22/78'.254
The 7% remnantThe company withholds 7% from a natural person's dividend that is exempt at company level under § 50(1^1) and whose underlying profit was taxed under the 14/86 rule in force until 31 December 2024. Per the Board a tax treaty may free a non-resident natural person from it (Cyprus is on the Board's list) or cut it to 5%.Income Tax Act § 61(68), transitional.254255
Dividends in place of pay (an owner who is also board member or employee)Per the Board, 'the dividend may not replace salary'. Pay at or near the industry norm: no tax risk. Pay significantly lower or at minimum wage, profit going out as dividends: a risk needing detailed analysis; if a dividend is found to have replaced salary, a basis to reclassify it as salary and apply social tax and payments.Board page only, last updated 1 November 2023, framed as general practice, not a binding assessment. Estonian law does not require a private limited company to pay its board member (Commercial Code § 180^1); the Board reads Law of Obligations Act § 28(1) as presuming that, unless otherwise agreed, a board member can expect pay.295297
Loan to a shareholderThe company pays income tax on a loan to its shareholder, partner or member 'if the circumstances of the transaction indicate that it may be a hidden profit distribution'.Income Tax Act § 50^2 (in force since 1 January 2018, act RT I, 07.07.2017, 3). For a loan with a repayment term over 48 months to a parent or a fellow subsidiary (not the lender's own subsidiary) the taxpayer must, at the tax authority's request, prove the ability and intention to repay, with at least 30 days for evidence.274
Paying a non-resident: code and treaty rateTo declare a payment to a non-resident on form TSD, or a dividend recipient on form INF 1, the company needs the recipient's Estonian code: personal identification code, registry code or, failing those, a non-resident code applied for by the recipient or the payer, which the Board says it issues 'within 10 days'.Per the Board, read 5 October 2026. An e-resident already has a personal identification code. The code does not open treaty rates: for those the Board must also hold a certificate of residence from the foreign tax authority (form TM3 or equivalent), valid 12 months for a natural person, 36 for a legal person.537

Source trap

Riigi Teataja's English translations lag the Estonian text as of 5 October 2026: the latest English Income Tax Act is the version in force 9 May-30 September 2026 and the latest English Value-Added Tax Act the version in force 1 January-30 September 2026, both shown as no longer in force; the texts in force from 1 October 2026 have no translation yet. The 1 October changes touch only Income Tax Act § 19 and Value-Added Tax Act § 39, so rates, exemptions and deadlines read the same in both languages.556

Open look again by 5 Apr 2027

Not established: whether the monthly minimum base of social tax (EUR 886 in 2026) applies to a board member's fee below it when the member has no other pay. Social Tax Act § 2(2) names employees and officials, and a Board example of an e-resident board member on EUR 100 a month gives EUR 33 social tax; but no sentence stating it outright was found in § 2(2)-(3), in the Board's TSD handbook example 14 (a member who also has a full salary) or on its social tax and tax rates pages.284291

Open look again by 5 Apr 2027

Under Value-Added Tax Act § 21 a company not registered for VAT that receives a listed service from a foreign business not registered for VAT in Estonia registers as a 'taxable person with limited liability' and pays the VAT itself. Not established: whether that holds when the only such service is VAT-exempt, for example a payment service from an acquirer in another member state. Looked for in the Act and on the Board's page 'Registration as VAT payer'.271557

Open look again by 12 Oct 2026

Pending on 5 October 2026: amendments to bills 992 SE and 994 SE, due by 12 October 2026; publication of the act adopted from bill 935 SE; the 2027 State Budget bill, which stands as introduced.285551553554

Open look again by 5 Jan 2027

A section-by-section comparison, made in this study, of the text in force with the text enacted for that date finds § 15 among the sections that differ (§§ 2, 15, 26, 27, 28 and 30); the records establish only that the standard rate of § 15(1) stays 24%, with no end date.262268

Open look again by 5 Apr 2027

Open: why these changes were made. The records read the acts in Riigi Teataja for the date and text of each change, and none states a reason: not for the VAT rises of 2024 and 2025, the security tax, its repeal, or the 24% income tax rate and its cancellation. Of the repeal only the timing is explained: 'not earlier than 2 July 2025', after the VAT rise had entered into force.262308309310

Not established in this section

  • The VAT return of 2027: 1 January or April. Why does the Tax and Customs Board date the new VAT return to April 2027 when the enacted rewrite of § 27 of the Value-Added Tax Act and the minister's regulation start on 1 January 2027? look again by 5 Jan 2027
  • The pension-pillar act of 30 September 2026. Has the act adopted from bill 935 SE been proclaimed and published, and with which commencement date? look again by 1 Nov 2026
  • Tax bills before the Riigikogu for 2027. Will bills 992 SE and 994 SE and the 2027 State Budget bill be adopted as introduced? look again by 1 Dec 2026
  • The treaty's dividend article and the company-level tax. How does Estonia apply Article 10(1) of the Estonia-Cyprus convention to the income tax a company pays on distributed profit when the shareholder is a Cyprus resident? look again by 5 Apr 2027
  • VAT registration when the only foreign service is exempt. Must an Estonian company that is not VAT-registered register as a taxable person with limited liability when the only service it receives from abroad is an exempt payment service, for example from an acquirer established in another member state? look again by 5 Apr 2027
  • Tax refunds to an account outside an Estonian bank. Does the Tax and Customs Board in practice refund overpaid tax to an IBAN at a payment or e-money institution, and can e-MTA initiate a payment from an account outside Estonia? look again by 5 Apr 2027

What is known on each point, and where it was looked for, is in the register at the end.

Question 7 · KYC for a person and for a company

KYC: the act lists what a bank must establish; the 'connection with Estonia' test is not in the act

The anti-money-laundering act makes an institution identify the customer, its representative and its beneficial owner and understand the purpose of the relationship; the phrase 'connection with Estonia' is not in it. Each of the four banks read on 5 October 2026 publishes such a test, and a non-refundable fee for a company with a non-resident owner.

Estonia's anti-money-laundering statute is the Rahapesu ja terrorismi rahastamise tõkestamise seadus (RahaPTS, Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Prevention Act), in force from 27 November 2017. Sections are cited from the consolidated text RT I, 11.07.2026, 93, in force from 21 July 2026.127

What RahaPTS and the supervisor's guidelines make an institution establish

  • Due diligence applies when a business relationship is established, for an occasional transaction from EUR 15,000, when earlier data are in doubt and on any suspicion of money laundering (§ 19(1)).558
  • The six measures of § 20(1): identify the customer and verify the data from a reliable independent source; identify the representative and his powers; identify the beneficial owner and understand the ownership and control structure; understand the purpose of the relationship; establish whether the person is politically exposed; monitor the relationship.558
  • For the purpose, the institution identifies 'inter alia, the permanent seat, place of business or place of residence, profession or field of activity, main contracting partners, payment habits' (§ 20(2)). The phrase 'connection with Estonia' occurs nowhere in the act's Estonian or English text (full-text search, 5 October 2026).90
  • A natural person is identified on an Estonian identity document listed in § 2(2) of the Identity Documents Act, a valid travel document issued in a foreign country, a driving licence meeting § 4(1) of that Act, or a birth certificate for a child under seven (§ 21); a legal person on its registry card or an equivalent document, not demanded where the bank itself has access to the relevant register (§ 22).94559
  • The beneficial owner (§ 9, wording in force since 20 July 2020) is the natural person with the final dominant influence through ownership or other control, or in whose interests a transaction is made; if that test gives no answer, the natural person whose direct and indirect shareholding together 'exceeds 25 per cent', bearer shares included; failing that, the senior managing official.136
  • Enhanced due diligence is mandatory in six cases (§ 36(2)), among them a politically exposed person (from or living in an EEA state: only with a higher-risk factor, § 38(5)) and a customer from, resident or seated in a high-risk third country. Risk-raising factors a bank must weigh (§ 37(2)-(3)) include a relationship started without meeting the customer or representative in person and without the § 31 safeguard.152560561
  • § 10: senior management sets the risk appetite in writing, naming at least the kinds of persons with whom the institution wishes to avoid business relationships. A refusal on this ground is a policy the act makes the bank set, not a statutory ban.562
  • § 42 forbids establishing a business relationship when the bank is unable to apply due diligence or suspects money laundering. § 43(2): it may terminate 'extraordinarily and without advance notification' when an e-resident's digital identity card is refused, suspended or declared invalid.563
  • Finantsinspektsioon's advisory guidelines (resolution of 26 November 2018 as amended on 8 April 2024; valid from 1 May 2024, comply-or-explain) keep in Annex 1, among customer risks in payment services, that 'the person has no connection with Estonia, but they still want to receive the service in Estonia'.91
  • Point 4.3.1.14 of the same guidelines: the data taken at identification must be verified face-to-face or by video identification if the customer is from a country outside the European Economic Area (EEA) or has their residence or place of business there, or if outgoing payments exceed EUR 15,000 per calendar month for a natural person or EUR 25,000 for a legal entity.564
  • A high-risk third country (§ 3 clause 18) is a country named in the Commission's delegated act under Article 9(2) of Directive (EU) 2015/849; the act holds no national list. The Financial Intelligence Unit separately publishes a 'List of Countries with a Higher Risk of Terrorist Financing' (appendix to its guidelines of 18 September 2024, still linked on 5 October 2026): 32 jurisdictions, reviewed once a year.140141

Remote identification and the e-resident in the act

SinceWasNowAct
10 Sep 2020Original text, in force 27 November 2017: § 31 made 'information technology means' mandatory when a relationship was opened without presence with an e-resident or a person from or living in a non-EEA country, and with an EEA person whose outgoing payments exceeded EUR 15,000 a month (legal person 25,000); § 10(4) had the management board of a credit or financial institution decide whether to deal with persons from outside the EEA 'or with e-residents'.The e-resident trigger was dropped from § 31; the words 'or with e-residents' were deleted from § 10(4).RT I, 10.07.2020, 1562565
1 Jul 2024Until 30 June 2024 § 31 applied to a person from or living in a non-EEA country (origin counted) and above the payment thresholds, its original text accepting only an Estonian digital ID or an eIDAS scheme of level 'high'; § 31(4) made every foreign national add an identity document issued by the foreign state. Minister of Finance regulation No 25 of 23.05.2018 (in force from 28 May 2018) required high-assurance e-identification, a questionnaire and a mandatory recorded real-time interview; its § 10 had the questionnaire establish 'the connection of the person's economic or family interests with Estonia'.When the customer is not met in person and lives or is seated outside the EEA (no longer origin), or outgoing payments exceed EUR 15,000 a month (natural person) or EUR 25,000 (legal person), the institution must use an eIDAS-notified electronic identification scheme of assurance level substantial or high, or a qualified trust service; failing that, another means securing correct data and recorded image, video and audio of sufficient quality (§ 31(3^1)) - a fallback closed to a customer living or seated in a high-risk third country or a jurisdiction supporting terrorism. Whom the banks say they do not take

  • LHV (FAQ read 5 October 2026, undated): 'We have decided to stop onboarding companies whose beneficial owners are Russian or Belarusian citizens without a valid Estonian residence permit.' Its page for non-residents: no accounts for companies registered offshore or owned through offshore companies.143573
  • Coop Pank (risk-appetite summary, last update 28.02.2025): no client relationships with Russian or Belarusian citizens without a valid residence permit of an EU or EEA state or Switzerland, nor with companies in which such a person holds 50% or more or controls the management or supervisory board as chairman.144
  • SEB's General Terms (01.06.2026) let it refuse or cancel without notice where the person 'has no legal basis for residing in the European Union' (refusal only), where a business client's beneficial owner from a country with a significant risk of corruption, terrorist financing, money laundering or sanctions has no legal right to reside in the EU, or for offshore residence or entities in its structure or partners.393
  • Swedbank's General Conditions effective 31 August 2026 list as a good reason to refuse a person with an offshore entity in its structure or among its partners (3.4.10); since that date the bank may also require proof of 'the lawful right to live in the European Union' (3.4).122579

The basic payment account and the refusals

  • § 710^1 of the Law of Obligations Act (Võlaõigusseadus), there since 10 January 2017: a credit institution must provide basic payment services to 'a consumer who lawfully resides in the European Union' and, 'in the case of justified interest of a consumer', conclude the contract; it answers within ten settlement days and must refuse on RahaPTS § 42(1) grounds. Only consumers hold the right: a company has none.108
  • Eesti Pank's Payments Review of March 2026 counts 4,237 refused applications to open a basic payment account at all banks in Estonia in 2025, an estimated 3.6% of applications: 2,628 concerned legal persons (2,578 resident, 50 non-resident) and 1,609 natural persons (1,099 resident, 510 non-resident).581
  • Relative to applications, non-resident legal persons were refused most often - 'nearly a third of applications'. Reasons banks gave for legal persons include a mismatch with the bank's risk appetite, an insufficient connection with Estonia and an opaque ownership structure.581

Refused applications to open a basic payment account at all banks in Estonia in 2025, by applicant (Eesti Pank, Payments Review of March 2026)

Basic payment account: the 2025 amendment

SinceWasNowAct
15 Jul 2025§ 710^1(2), wording in force from 10 January 2017 to 14 July 2025: a credit institution had to conclude the contract with a consumer lawfully residing in the EU who had a justified interest 'proceeding from the conditions provided for in subsection 1' - that is, where the person met the law and the bank's general terms.That reference is removed; proving justified interest 'must not be unreasonably burdensome' (§ 710^1(2^1)). The bank 'may not refuse to enter into a basic payment service contract on the basis of the risk appetite' set under § 10 of RahaPTS (§ 710^1(5^1)), but may mitigate risk by capping monthly payments, single transactions and the balance and by restricting cash withdrawals outside the EEA; the limits do not cover payments of taxes, fines or state fees and may not make the account unusable.RT I, 08.07.2025, 4109582
Source trap

A company registered in Estonia is not thereby a customer of an Estonian bank: in an outline assembled from the records of the four banks read on 5 October 2026, registration alone is nowhere listed as enough for a company with a non-resident owner or board member. The state e-Residency programme's article 'Banking basics' (updated 21 April 2026) tells e-residents that access to business banking 'is not guaranteed'.92583

Source trap

Eesti Pank's review counts refused basic-payment-account applications from legal persons as well (2,628 of the 4,237 in 2025), while the right of § 710^1 of the Law of Obligations Act belongs to consumers only. The two statements come from different sources and are shown as each stands.108581

Question 8 · Registration, audit, people to hire

State fee EUR 265 expedited, EUR 200 otherwise; the Accounting Act requires no accountant

As of 5 October 2026 the state fee for entering a private limited company (OÜ) is EUR 200, or EUR 265 on an expedited-procedure application; texts published up to 1 July 2027 keep both. A notary, contact person and auditor are required only in stated cases; the Accounting Act requires no accountant. Vendors' prices are their own claims.

Official fees, as in force on 5 October 2026

FeeAmountAct, and since when
Entering an OÜ in the commercial registerEUR 200; EUR 265 when the entry is made on an expedited-procedure applicationState Fees Act (Riigilõivuseadus) § 62(2); both amounts since 1 January 2022 (RT I, 08.12.2021, 1). The texts published for 1 November 2026, 1 January 2027 and 1 July 2027 keep them.11376
Entering a sole proprietor (FIE)EUR 20State Fees Act § 62(1); unchanged since 1 January 2022 and in the versions enacted up to 1 July 2027586
Entering a change for an OÜEUR 25; double for an entry made on a fixed dateState Fees Act § 63(2), amount in force since 1 January 2022 (RT I, 08.12.2021, 1); § 63(4), since 1 March 2024587588
Reserving a business name (optional)EUR 150; one name, for six monthsState Fees Act § 62¹ and Commercial Code § 15¹, since 1 March 202410588
Restoring a legal person deleted for a missing annual report or missing contact personEUR 200State Fees Act § 62(4), from 1 February 2023588
Examining an application for an e-resident's digital identity cardEUR 150; not refunded if the application is left unexaminedState Fees Act § 272(6), wording of RT I, 20.12.2024, 1, in force from 1 January 2025; § 15¹ p 1. The founder section gives the rest.589
Notary: founding outside the expedited procedureElements only. One 'full fee' for a unilateral act, twice the full fee for an act of two or more parties; the full fee for a value up to EUR 128 is EUR 6. Drafting the application to the registration department, certifying the signature and a digital copy: EUR 35.75, plus EUR 6.35 for each further signatory. VAT is added.Notary Fees Act § 18(1), (5), § 22, § 23, § 31 p 9¹, as in force on 5 October 2026. The transaction value is the planned share capital, but not less than EUR 127 nor more than EUR 639,116.73
Notary: remote authentication by video linkEUR 20 before VAT added to the fee (the Chamber of Notaries' Estonian page: EUR 24.80 with VAT; its English page: EUR 24)Notary Fees Act § 2(2¹); remote authentication since 1 February 2020 (Notarisation Act § 1(3¹), § 13(7)), at the notary's discretion, signing with an Estonian ID-card, digital ID, Smart-ID, Mobile-ID, residence-permit card or e-resident's digital ID.590

The Tax and Customs Board's comparison of business forms (updated 14 May 2026) sets two forms for a natural person beside the OÜ. An entrepreneur-account holder files no returns and cannot deduct expenses; 'currently, entrepreneur accounts can only be opened at the LHV Pank', though the Simplified Business Income Taxation Act allows any EEA-resident credit institution; above EUR 40,000 received on the account in a calendar year the person must register as a sole proprietor or OÜ and for VAT. A sole proprietor (FIE) applies for entry before starting activity (Commercial Code § 3) and has no annual-report duty (Accounting Act § 43); Code § 135(2) limits liability for OÜ shareholders only.586591592

What is advertised: the e-Residency programme's own figures and four vendors' claims (pages read 5 October 2026)

e-Residency programme, page 'Start a company'Contact person EUR 200-400 a year; registering the company EUR 265; bank account EUR 0-200The programme's own cost and time figures: contact person 1 day - 1 week; registration online 15 min - 1 hour; bank account online 1 hour - 1 week.86

The article 'strongly recommend[s]' a professional accountant.593594

Whom the law makes an OÜ engage, as of 5 October 2026 (outline assembled from the records)

WhoRequired by law whenInstrument
NotaryTo found the company outside the expedited procedure; to transfer or pledge a share unless the articles waive the notarial form; to certify a register application not signed digitally, unless a foreign official certifies the signatures (then legalised or with an apostille, unless a treaty provides otherwise)Commercial Code § 138(4)-(5), § 149(4), (6); Commercial Register Act § 40(1). Contact personOnly if the company's address is in a foreign state; then only a notary, advocate, law-office operator, sworn auditor, audit firm, non-resident's tax representative or trust and company service provider may be appointedCommercial Register Act § 24, in force from 1 February 2023; the list is § 24(4). Providing trust and company services requires an activity licence decided by the Financial Intelligence Unit (Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Prevention Act § 70(1) p 2, § 71(1)).7072598
AuditorFor the annual accounts only above the audit and review thresholds or if the articles require; and to check a contribution in kind from EUR 25,000Auditors Activities Act §§ 91-92 and Commercial Code § 190(1) for the annual accounts; the in-kind check per the synthesis, which cites Commercial Code § 143(3)16598
AccountantNot under the Accounting Act: it does not say who keeps the books, has no occurrence of 'raamatupidaja' (accountant) and no licensing or qualification condition for bookkeeping. The board organises the accounting; neither Commercial Code § 183 nor § 179(1) requires an accountant. Sector laws for licensed activities not checked.Accounting Act, consolidated text in force on 5 October 2026; Commercial Code § 183, § 179(1)4647598

A supervisory board is needed only if the articles prescribe one. Sector laws for licensed activities were not checked.Commercial Code §§ 135-220, § 189(1), text in force on 5 October 20261516598
Source trap

The EUR 265 on the e-Residency programme's pages is, in State Fees Act § 62(2), the expedited-procedure fee; the ordinary fee is EUR 200. Unicount's 'in 1 working day' and Eesti Firma's 'usually approves the company within one business day' are vendors' claims: by law review is due no later than the next working day in the expedited procedure and within five working days otherwise; a period restarts when the application is amended or a further document is filed (Commercial Register Act §§ 41(3)-(4), 42(2)).1387594596600

2015–2026 · What changed and when

2015 to 2026 and the dates already fixed: a bank clean-up, tax rises, a new register act

Each event carries the date of effect where a record gives one and names the act or the body. Entries dated after 5 October 2026 are dates set in adopted acts, EU regulations, published notices or bills still before the Riigikogu (the parliament); a proposed date is marked as proposed. Events resting on press reports or on a firm's own notice say so.

2015

1 Jan 2015
Tax
Income tax rate falls from 21% to 20% § 4 of the Income Tax Act, enacted in advance by RT I, 08.07.2011, 5: one rate for persons and for companies' distributed profit, the latter grossed up by dividing by 0.80.245541

2017

21 Nov 2017
Payments
First Estonian bank ready for SEPA instant credit transfers The European Payments Council's register (read 5 October 2026) dates the readiness of AS SEB Pank to 21 November 2017, the scheme's first day, Swedbank to 11 September 2018 and AS LHV Pank to 20 November 2018. Eesti Pank's page: 'The first Estonian banks joined the instant payment system in 2018'.174601
27 Nov 2017
AML and sanctions
A new anti-money-laundering act enters into force The Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Prevention Act (the AML act), passed 26 October 2017 (RT I, 17.11.2017, 2). §§ 76-80 on beneficial owners followed on 1 September 2018, §§ 81 and 95 on 1 January 2019. The first virtual-currency licences were issued under it in late autumn 2017.127602603

2018

1 Jan 2018
Tax
A 14% rate for regular dividends starts; it lasts seven years Income Tax Act § 4(5), RT I, 07.07.2017, 3: profit distributed up to the average of the previous three years' taxed distributions bore 14% (divisor 0.86), with a further 7% withheld on such dividends paid to natural persons. The rate first applied in 2019. Both were repealed from 1 January 2025.542604
1 Jan 2018
Tax
VAT registration threshold rises from EUR 16,000 to EUR 40,000 Value-Added Tax Act § 19(1). The amount is unchanged on 5 October 2026; the way it is counted changed on 1 January 2025, when the turnover of § 19^1(3) replaced taxable turnover and brought in real-estate, insurance and financial-services turnover unless incidental.547
13 Jan 2018
Payments
Second Payment Services Directive (PSD2) transposed; card surcharges banned On the directive's deadline RT I, 07.12.2017, 1 amended the Payment Institutions and E-money Institutions Act and the Law of Obligations Act, whose § 725(9) bars a payee's fee for instruments with regulated interchange. The Government's memorandum rates the effect on traders as small.179526605
15 Jan 2018
Company law
A company run from abroad must appoint a contact person in Estonia Commercial Code § 63^1: a company whose board 'is located in a foreign state' names a listed professional, or a board member, shareholder or procurist living in Estonia. Before, only an OÜ with half its board or more outside Estonia, the EEA and Switzerland named a person with an Estonian address.71331
26 Mar 2018
Banks
European Central Bank (ECB) withdraws the licence of Versobank AS Proposed by Finantsinspektsioon, the supervisor, on 8 February 2018: significant, long-lasting breaches, above all of AML rules. A decision of 17 July 2018 replaced it; the Court of Justice dismissed the appeal on 7 September 2023. Per the Guarantee Fund, EUR 90.2 million went to 2,571 depositors.147606607608
1 Sep 2018
Company law
The beneficial-owner register starts §§ 76-80 of the AML act entered into force on this date under § 119(1); companies file through the commercial register information system. § 78(1), worded as since 7 March 2022, still says on 5 October 2026 that beneficial-owner data and the notations on them 'are made public' in that system.602
19 Sep 2018
AML and sanctions
Danske Bank reports about EUR 200 billion of flows through its Estonian branch Its press release on the Bruun & Hjejle investigation: for around 15,000 customers 'with non-resident characteristics' the 'total flow of payments amounted to around EUR 200 billion' over 2007-2015. The bank is 'not able to provide an accurate estimate of the amount of suspicious transactions'.609

2019

1 Jan 2019
Company law
Share capital may be paid into an account anywhere in the EEA Commercial Code § 520(4) as amended by RT I, 20.12.2018, 1: 'a payment account in a credit institution or payment institution' established in a state of the European Economic Area (EEA) replaced 'a bank account in an Estonian credit institution'. E-money institutions are not named.105610
2 Jan 2019
Banks
Luminor becomes one Estonian bank with branches in Latvia and Lithuania Luminor, combining the Baltic operations of DNB and Nordea, started on 1 October 2017. Per the bank's own notice the cross-border merger, approved by the ECB on 28 June 2018, put deposits at the Latvian and Lithuanian branches under the Estonian Guarantee Fund from 2 January 2019.611
19 Feb 2019
Banks
Finantsinspektsioon orders Danske Bank's Estonian branch to close A precept of its management board: the branch had to cease its activities in Estonia within eight months, return all deposits and transfer its loan contracts. The supervisor's report for 2019 lists the termination with the date 02.12.2019; AS LHV Pank acquired the loans to private clients.148612
29 Apr 2019
Payments
Two payment institutions lose their licences Finantsinspektsioon withdrew the licences of AS Talveaed, whose model 'focused on providing payment services to foreign resident clients' and which had served them despite a January 2018 ban, and on 27 May 2019 that of GFC Good Finance Company AS for 'serious breaches of the law'.463
14 Sep 2019
Payments
Strong customer authentication takes effect § 5(2) of the transposing Act (RT I, 07.12.2017, 1) put the Law of Obligations Act provisions into force 18 months after the Commission's technical standard. For card payments in e-commerce, per Finantsinspektsioon's circular of 3 December 2020, the extra migration period ended on 31 December 2020.613

2020

1 Feb 2020
Company law
Notaries may authenticate acts remotely by video link Notarisation Act § 1(3^1), § 13(7), Act RT I, 22.02.2019, 2: all parties sign digitally. Per the Chamber of Notaries it is at the notary's discretion and it names only Estonian signing tools, the e-resident's digital ID among them. The Notary Fees Act as of 5 October 2026 adds EUR 20 before VAT.55590
10 Mar 2020
AML and sanctions
Virtual-currency licensing tightened, and again on 15 March 2022 Act RT I, 31.12.2019, 2 (AML act and State Fees Act): seat, board and place of business in Estonia (or an Estonian branch), capital at least EUR 12,000, fee EUR 3,300 (EUR 345 before); RT I, 12.03.2022, 2: capital at least EUR 100,000, or EUR 250,000 for the transfer service, fee EUR 10,000.614615616
19 Mar 2020
AML and sanctions
Precept to Swedbank AS; the SEK 4 billion fine is Swedish Sweden's Finansinspektionen fined Swedbank AB SEK 4 billion. Finantsinspektsioon issued a precept to Swedbank AS and announced no Estonian fine. It fined AS SEB Pank EUR 1 million on 25 June 2020 and AS LHV Pank EUR 900,000 on 19 October 2023.617618
1 Jul 2020
Residence and work
Visa for remote work (the 'digital nomad visa') in force Aliens Act § 62^5: a short- or long-stay visa for an alien whose work does not depend on location and who works for an employer registered abroad, for a company registered abroad in which he or she has a holding, or serves clients mainly abroad. It gives a temporary stay, not a residence permit.370
29 Jul 2020
Residence and work
National Audit Office audits the controls of e-Residency Its audit concluded that 'the current ex-ante and ex-post controls do not ensure that the participants of the programme are law-abiding'. From 1 April 2021 an amended regulation of the Minister of the Interior makes applicants supply additional data, per the Police and Border Guard Board.619

2021

1 Jan 2021
AML and sanctions
The Financial Intelligence Unit leaves the police In the 2017 wording the Unit (Rahapesu Andmebüroo) was a structural unit of the Police and Border Guard Board. Acts RT I, 10.07.2020, 1 and RT I, 21.11.2020, 1 made it a governmental authority under the Ministry of Finance, with its own budget and a head appointed by the Government for five years.527

2022

1 Jan 2022
Company law
State fee for registering a company: EUR 200, or EUR 265 expedited State Fees Act § 62(2) as amended by RT I, 08.12.2021, 1; it set EUR 145 and EUR 190 from 1 January 2015 to 31 December 2021. Unchanged on 5 October 2026 and in the texts published for 1 November 2026, 1 January 2027 and 1 July 2027.11620
11 Mar 2022
Residence and work
First-time e-Residency stopped for Russian and Belarusian citizens Per the Police and Border Guard Board in court, its Director General signed order No 1.1-4.11/12 after the cabinet took note on 8 March 2022; no list was published. Tallinn Administrative Court dismissed a complaint on 18 May 2023 (case 3-22-858): the card is a benefit, not a right.339354621
28 Mar 2022
Payments
Maksekeskus purchase by Luminor cleared; LHV had announced buying EveryPay The Competition Authority cleared Luminor Bank's purchase of 99% of Maksekeskus (contract of 31 January 2022). LHV Group had announced on 4 March 2022 a deal to buy EveryPay AS, subject to its general meeting of 30 March; on 5 October 2026 the register shows it as AS LHV Paytech, owned by LHV Group.202457
8 Apr 2022
Banks
Extra deposit cover for natural persons raised and widened Guarantee Fund Act § 25(2^1), RT I, 29.03.2022, 3. Before: up to EUR 70,000 above the EUR 100,000 limit for home-sale money. Since: up to a further EUR 100,000 credited in the six months before deposits became unavailable, from a home sale, marriage, divorce, dismissal and other listed events.622
9 Apr 2022
Residence and work
Sanction regulation restricts visas and residence permits Government Regulation No 42 of 8 April 2022 (International Sanctions Act). Version in force since 21 May 2023: Russian citizens applying for any visa or a temporary residence permit for study, employment or enterprise; Belarusian citizens for listed visas and permits; exceptions in § 2(3) and (6).371372
13 Dec 2022
AML and sanctions
Danske Bank: resolutions of USD 2.06 billion with US and Danish authorities Over its former Estonian branch: 'final coordinated resolutions' with the US Department of Justice, the US SEC and Denmark's Special Crime Unit, with a guilty plea in the United States. Per the public broadcaster ERR, six former employees were charged in Estonia on 17 April 2023.623624

2023

1 Feb 2023
Company law
Commercial Register Act in force: minimum capital and unpaid founding end Äriregistri seadus, RT I, 05.05.2022, 1. The same publication removed the EUR 2,500 minimum capital of a private limited company (OÜ); founders must pay for their shares in full before filing; the contact-person duty now turns on the company's address being abroad, with no insider exception.2315329330331625
1 Feb 2023
Company law
Deletion for a missing annual report becomes faster Commercial Register Act § 61: the registrar may delete at least three months after the filing date, after a notice; before, a report six months overdue opened a time limit of at least six months. Per ERR, supervision deleted over 31,000 entities in 2024; the register links the jump to the Act.333626
1 Nov 2023
AML and sanctions
Fines under the AML act are raised RT I, 17.03.2023, 2. Failing to identify a customer cost a legal person 'up to 400 000 euros' in the 2017 text; now up to EUR 1,000,000 or twice the benefit and, under Finantsinspektsioon's supervision, up to EUR 5,000,000, twice the benefit or 10 per cent of a legal person's consolidated turnover.149

2024

1 Jan 2024
Tax
Standard VAT rate rises from 20% to 22% Value-Added Tax Act § 15(1), amended by RT I, 01.07.2023, 2; the 20% rate had applied since 1 July 2009. The same Act moved accommodation from 9% to 13% and press publications from 5% to 9% on 1 January 2025.262530548
29 Feb 2024
AML and sanctions
Estonian criminal investigation into Swedbank AS ends without charges Swedbank's release: the State Prosecutor's Office's investigation 'shows that no crime has been committed'. Per the bank it concerned 2014-2016 (per ERR 2011-2016); a suspicion had been served on 24 March 2022. ERR: the money's criminal origin could not be proven, though rule breaches were found.627628
1 Jul 2024
AML and sanctions
Remote identification rewritten: residence outside the EEA is the trigger AML act § 31: a customer not met in person living or seated outside the EEA, or paying out over EUR 15,000 a month (legal person 25,000), needs eIDAS level substantial or high, a qualified trust service or, failing that, a recorded procedure. Before, origin also counted and 'substantial' did not.133565

2025

1 Jan 2025
Tax
Income tax rate rises to 22%; the 14% dividend rate ends Act of 20 June 2023 (RT I, 30.06.2023, 107): 22% for persons and 22/78 on companies' distributed profit; the lower rate for regular dividends and its 7% withholding are repealed. Under § 61(68) 7% is still withheld on some dividends paid out of profit taxed under the old rule.245541604
1 Jan 2025
Payments
Cash payments at points of sale are rounded to five cents § 5-1 of the Act on the Introduction of the Euro and the Settlement of Cash Payments in Euros, inserted by RT I, 30.05.2024, 2. Card and other non-cash payments are not rounded. The Consumer Protection and Technical Regulatory Authority supervises the rule.510
1 Jan 2025
Residence and work
The e-Residency state fee becomes EUR 150 State Fees Act § 272(6): EUR 50 from 1 December 2014 (EUR 80 at a foreign mission from 1 April 2015); EUR 100 from 1 February 2016; EUR 100, or EUR 130 at a foreign mission, from 6 January 2020; EUR 150 from 1 January 2025. The texts published for dates up to 1 July 2027 keep EUR 150.378589629
17 Jan 2025
Company law
Audit and review thresholds raised Auditors Activities Act as amended by RT I, 07.01.2025, 1, applied retroactively to reporting periods begun from 1 January 2024. Audit, two-of-three test: EUR 5m revenue, EUR 2.5m assets, 50 employees (before: EUR 4m, EUR 2m, 50). Review: EUR 2m, EUR 1m, 24 (before: EUR 1.6m, EUR 0.8m, 24).323324
14 Apr 2025
Banks
AS TBB pank leaves the market; eight licensed banks remain Finantsinspektsioon let it end its services voluntarily, which revoked the licence. From 19 February 2024 a precept citing major deficiencies in money-laundering prevention had barred it from cross-border payment services for non-resident legal entities or those with a non-resident beneficial owner.630
1 Jul 2025
Tax
Standard VAT rate rises from 22% to 24% § 21 of the Security Tax Act (RT I, 02.01.2025, 2). It was enacted with a return to 22% on 1 January 2029; that clause fell with the Act's repeal on 18 July 2025. The text in force on 5 October 2026 and the one published for 1 January 2027 state 24% with no end date.262530
1 Jul 2025
Company law
A business marked as e-invoice recipient may require e-invoices Accounting Act § 7^1, RT I, 10.10.2024, 1: a buyer marked as e-invoice recipient in the commercial register may require the seller to present an e-invoice. From 1 July 2019 the duty covered supplies to the public sector and entities it controls. No general duty to e-invoice every business customer.631
6 Jul 2025
Residence and work
A citizenship ground for returning e-Residency applications enters the Act § 20^6(1^1) of the Identity Documents Act (RT I, 26.06.2025, 1). The Government's memorandum says background checks alone do not reduce risk enough for states without judicial, security or law-enforcement cooperation. The Financial Intelligence Unit joined the bodies supervising card use.79632633
15 Jul 2025
Banks
A bank may not refuse a consumer a basic payment account on risk appetite Act RT I, 08.07.2025, 4 amended § 710^1 of the Law of Obligations Act: no refusal on the ground of the bank's risk appetite, and proof of justified interest must not be unreasonably burdensome. In the wording in force from 10 January 2017 the right was tied to the bank's own terms.109582
15 Jul 2025
Payments
Banks must enable offline card payments in an emergency § 87-1 of the Credit Institutions Act, added by RT I, 08.07.2025, 4, on conditions set by Eesti Pank; it lays no duty on merchants. The same Act capped the fee a payee may agree for instruments outside the surcharge ban at the payee's actual cost.180528
18 Jul 2025
Payments
Payment and e-money institutions may join settlement systems Act RT I, 08.07.2025, 5: a non-exempt institution may be a system member once Finantsinspektsioon recognises its compliance, and may keep client funds at Eesti Pank; but ECB Decision (EU) 2025/222, applying from 9 April 2025, bars Eurosystem central banks from offering such safeguarding accounts.634635
18 Aug 2025
Payments
Licence of Ühisarveldused AS withdrawn Finantsinspektsioon acted for material breaches of the rules governing its activity and of anti-money-laundering requirements. A court suspended the decision on 8 September 2025 and, by a ruling announced on 19 May 2026, lifted that relief; per the May 2026 notice the main proceedings continue.464
1 Oct 2025
Residence and work

3 Dec 2025
Tax
Both income-tax rises enacted for 2026 are cancelled before applying The Security Tax Act of 11 December 2024 set a 2% tax on personal income and company profit from 2026. An Act of 18 June 2025 repealed it from 18 July 2025 and set a 24% income tax rate instead; an Act of 3 December 2025, in force 1 January 2026, struck that out. The rate is 22% on 5 October 2026.308309310543

10 Jul 2027
AML and sanctions
EU AML Regulation applies: cash payments to traders capped at EUR 10,000 Article 80 of Regulation (EU) 2024/1624: persons trading in goods or providing services may accept or make a cash payment only up to EUR 10,000, in one operation or several linked ones. Member States may set lower limits; none was found in Estonian law on 5 October 2026.137

What the records leave open on 5 October 2026, and when each statement goes stale

Questions the Estonian records leave open or only narrow. Each item says what the records establish, where it was looked for, what would close it and when to look again. AML stands for anti-money-laundering, EEA for the European Economic Area, ECB for the European Central Bank, OÜ for a private limited company, eID for an electronic identity.

1 Nov 2026TaxesThe pension-pillar act of 30 September 2026
Has the act adopted from bill 935 SE been proclaimed and published, and with which commencement date?		Adopted by the Riigikogu on 30 September 2026 and sent to the President for promulgation on 1 October 2026; not published in Riigi Teataja as of 5 October 2026. Per the adopted text it enters into force on 1 November 2026, with listed points on 1 January 2028. From 1 November 2026 a person who stopped contributing to the second pension pillar, or withdrew money from it, may resume after five years instead of ten; from 1 January 2028 money may be withdrawn before pension age in part. It bears on payroll because resuming restarts withholding of the 2%, 4% or 6% contribution.551Publication of the act in Riigi Teataja.Looked: The Riigikogu proceeding record, read 5 October 2026; a title search of Riigi Teataja the same day found no published act.
1 Dec 2026TaxesTax bills before the Riigikogu for 2027
Will bills 992 SE and 994 SE and the 2027 State Budget bill be adopted as introduced?		Proposed, not adopted as of 5 October 2026. Bill 992 SE (Value-Added Tax Act) widens the one-stop-shop scheme and would end tax periods longer than a calendar month from 1 January 2027. Bill 994 SE, proposed to start on 1 December 2026, would make Estonian residents file the payroll return TSD electronically from 1 January 2027. Both completed first reading on 28 September 2026, amendments due by 12 October 2026; neither changes a tax rate. The 2027 State Budget bill (1037 SE, introduced 29 September 2026) sets the monthly rate for minimum social tax at EUR 946, up from EUR 886 in 2026.267285553554The Riigikogu records after the second and third readings, and the acts in Riigi Teataja.Looked: The Riigikogu proceeding records and the bill texts for first reading, read 5 October 2026.

5 Jan 2027CostsA notary's total fee for founding an OÜ
What does a notary charge in total for founding a typical small private limited company outside the expedited procedure?		Only the statutory elements are established (Notary Fees Act, in force on 5 October 2026): the transaction value is the planned share capital, not less than EUR 127; the fee is one full fee from the table for a unilateral act and twice that for an act of two or more parties, the full fee for a value up to EUR 128 being EUR 6; drafting the application to the registrar, certifying the signature and a digital copy cost a fixed EUR 35.75, plus EUR 6.35 for each further signatory; remote authentication adds EUR 20; VAT is added. No worked example was found.73590A fee example published by the Chamber of Notaries, or a notary's fee statement for such an act.Looked: Notary Fees Act §§ 2, 18, 22, 23, 29 and 31; the Chamber of Notaries' English pages.

5 Jan 2027Entry pointsThe court case over Ühisarveldused AS's licence
How will the administrative-court case over Finantsinspektsioon's withdrawal of Ühisarveldused AS's payment-institution licence end?		Finantsinspektsioon withdrew the licence of Ühisarveldused AS, third-largest Estonian payment institution by volume in 2024-2025, on 18 August 2025 for material breaches of the rules governing its activity and of AML requirements. Tallinn Administrative Court suspended the decision provisionally on 8 September 2025; by a ruling announced on 19 May 2026, open to appeal within 15 days, it lifted the interim relief, leaving the firm without a valid licence from then. Per the May 2026 notice the main proceedings continue; on 5 October 2026 the register lists the firm among closed institutions.464A search of Tallinn Administrative Court decisions in Riigi Teataja for Ühisarveldused AS; later notices on fi.ee.Looked: Finantsinspektsioon's notices of 8 September 2025 and 19 May 2026 and its register, on 5 October 2026; no case number is given and the court database was not searched.
5 Jan 2027Founder abroadBill 905 SE and the journey to collect the card
Will bill 905 SE be adopted, with which dates, and will an e-resident still have to travel to collect the card?		Pending on 5 October 2026: initiated by the Government on 7 May 2026, at the second-reading stage, not adopted. Per the Riigikogu's summary e-residents' identity would be verified remotely by biometrics from March 2027 and a card-free 'e-identification means' would replace the card from March 2028; the bill's § 9 sets 2 March 2027 and 1 March 2028. As tabled it leaves the EUR 150 fee. 5 Jan 2027Founder abroadThe money test of the remote-work visa
Which amount does the Police and Border Guard Board apply to a remote-work visa in 2026, and is it gross or net?		§ 15(7) of Minister of the Interior Regulation No 82 of 18 December 2015 sets sufficient funds per day of stay at 0.60 times the subsistence level since 1 January 2026, 0.75 times until 31 December 2025. With the subsistence level at EUR 220 a month in 2026 that is, by the study's arithmetic, EUR 132 a day, EUR 3,960 for 30 days; at EUR 200 in 2025 it was EUR 4,500 for 30 days - the figure the e-Residency programme's page still showed on 5 October 2026, as 'EUR 4,500 net/monthly'. The regulation does not say net or gross.370381The Board's own page or application form stating the amount for 2026, or its written answer.Looked: Regulation No 82, § 15(7), in the versions in force in 2023, 2025 and from 22 May 2026; the State Budget Acts for 2025 and 2026; the Board's long-term visa page (no amount); the programme's page on the visa.
