5 Jan 2027RulesWho may pull bank-account information
Which authorities are connected to the Enforcement Register for account queries, and will bill 924 SE be adopted?		§ 63(2) of the Code of Enforcement Procedure (from 23 February 2026) lets a 'holder of public authority' needing the Enforcement Register for a task arising from law join it, if technically able and linked to a tool showing people the queries about them; it gives no list. Since 1 January 2024 credit and financial institutions provide account holders, beneficial owners and IBANs through it. Per its official summary, bill 924 SE (initiated 21 May 2026, past first reading, pending on 5 October 2026) would specify bank-data rights of the Tax and Customs Board and the Financial Intelligence Unit.113114115The statute of the Enforcement Register in Riigi Teataja; the operator's list of connected authorities; the Riigikogu record after the second and third readings.Looked: Code of Enforcement Procedure § 63; AML act §§ 81 and 58; the Riigikogu register entry for bill 924 SE. The statute of the register and the operator's website were not read.
5 Jan 2027StagesThe registrar's processing times
How long does the registrar actually take to enter a company, in the expedited and in the ordinary procedure, as a published statistic?		As of 5 October 2026 the law gives the registrar five working days from the arrival of an application, a period that restarts when the application is amended, and the next working day in the expedited procedure; the registrar may extend it up to three months for special verification (Commercial Register Act § 41(3)-(5), § 42(2)). The e-Residency programme's knowledge base says the review 'usually takes one business day'; the Centre of Registers' help pages give '1-5 working days' for change applications. No measured figure per procedure was found.1385600A registrar or ministry report with median or average days per procedure type, or the dashboard figures read in a browser.Looked: The page of the registration department of Tartu County Court on kohus.ee; the commercial register page on justdigi.ee; the statistics pages of ariregister.rik.ee, whose charts are embedded dashboards; the e-Residency knowledge base - all on 5 October 2026.
5 Jan 2027StagesFines and deletions in practice
What fines does the registrar impose in practice for a late annual report, and how many private limited companies are entered and deleted each year?		The registrar may fine repeatedly, without a warning order, until the report is filed; a fine may be up to EUR 3,200 and, since 1 February 2023, the law names no minimum (Commercial Register Act § 57; Code of Civil Procedure § 46(1)). Per ERR (22 January 2026), 12,687 entities ('ettevõte') were deleted in 2025, over 4,550 of them for an unfiled annual report, and 28,329 were entered; the study infers the counts cover all legal forms. The register says over 20,000 private limited companies have been established annually since 2016. Typical fines and counts by legal form: not found.50332626638The dashboard figures read in a browser; the register's 'Fines' search exported for a period; the register's open-data files counted by legal form and status date.Looked: Commercial Register Act § 57; Code of Civil Procedure §§ 46 and 601; the statistics pages of ariregister.rik.ee, 'Fines and warnings' among them (text only; the charts are embedded dashboards); the ERR article of 22 January 2026.

Unchanged on 5 October 2026: a company page opened without logging in still showed the beneficial owner's name, personal identification code and manner of control. The restriction announced for 10 July 2026 was not applied; the Ministry of Finance said the date will be announced once the regulation is adopted, and per ERR (25 June 2026) the Minister of Justice and Digital Affairs had not approved the draft. Regulation No 22 of 6 April 2023, whose § 10(2) makes the data public, stands unamended. Applications for assessment of a legitimate interest can still be filed.36334639The adopted regulation in Riigi Teataja, or a dated notice of the Ministry.Looked: The Ministry of Finance's releases of 8 June and 10 July 2026 and its page on filing beneficial-owner data (updated 9 September 2026); the Centre of Registers' notices of 4 June and 1 July 2026; ERR; AML act § 78; a public company page on ariregister.rik.ee on 5 October 2026.
5 Jan 2027TaxesThe VAT return of 2027: 1 January or April
Why does the Tax and Customs Board date the new VAT return to April 2027 when the enacted rewrite of § 27 of the Value-Added Tax Act and the minister's regulation start on 1 January 2027?		Enacted and not yet in force on 5 October 2026: the rewrite of § 27 (RT I, 03.12.2024, 2) puts invoice data and intra-Community supply data on the return itself from 1 January 2027, and Minister of Finance Regulation No 19 of 28 September 2026 applies its four parts to taxable periods beginning from that date. The Board's page (updated 16 September 2026) says that from April 2027 VAT payers submit the transaction data behind the return instead of the return form, and leaves the three-month gap unexplained.268269549The amendments to bill 992 SE at second reading (due by 12 October 2026), or an explanation by the Board.Looked: The consolidated Value-Added Tax Act for 1 January 2027 (no transitional clause with an April date found); bills 992 SE and 994 SE as introduced; the Board's pages updated in September 2026.

Did the Financial Intelligence Unit cancel the remaining licences on 1 July 2026, and how many were valid at the end of 2022, 2023 and 2024?		The Unit gives 641 valid licences when it began its clean-up in mid-2021, 381 on 31 December 2021, 37 on 4 December 2025 and 36 at the start of 2026. 1 July 2026 was the deadline in § 48(4) of the Markets in Crypto-Assets Act for such providers to comply; a joint notice of 30 June 2026 said the Unit 'will consequently cancel on 1 July' the register entries of the remaining licences, and no confirmation was found by 5 October 2026. 5 Jan 2027TimelineThe Estonian trial over Danske Bank's branch
Has the criminal trial of six former employees of the branch ended in a judgment?		Per ERR, the Prosecutor's Office charged six former employees of the branch's foreign-banking unit on 17 April 2023 with large-scale money laundering in 2007-2015, and the trial started in Harju County Court on 13 November 2023. No judgment was found in web searches in English and Estonian on 5 October 2026. For the bank itself the case closed in stages, per its announcements: resolutions of 13 December 2022 with US and Danish authorities, with a US probation it says concluded on 15 December 2025, and a French settlement of 18 September 2024; civil litigation was left out of them.623624The Prosecutor's Office's press releases; the court decisions database in Riigi Teataja for Harju County Court.Looked: Web searches on 5 October 2026; the ERR reports of 17 April and 13 November 2023. OTP Bank announced on 20 July 2026 a signed agreement to acquire 100% of Luminor Holding; completion 'is subject to obtaining the necessary regulatory approvals'.438468469Completion notices from the banks, the ECB's decisions on the merger and on qualifying holdings, and Finantsinspektsioon's news.Looked: Finantsinspektsioon's notice of 22 May 2026; OTP Bank's announcement and DNB's release of 20 July 2026; SEB's index of terms; one web search on 5 October 2026.
5 Apr 2027Entry pointsWho acquires Estonia's card payments
What are the market shares of card acquirers, which foreign providers stand behind the terminal payments the ECB shows as acquired from abroad, and are the ECB series comparable across 2021 and 2022?		No figures by acquirer were found. The ECB shows terminal payments in Estonia on Estonian-issued cards 'acquired by non-resident PSPs' (payment service providers) at 61.5 million in 2022 and 147.3 million in 2025, counted on the issuing side, and does not name the acquirers. Its series for resident acquirers for 2022, the first year of the revised reporting, are not reconciled with its 'received domestic' card payments. An outline assembled from the records reads them two ways, putting the share acquired from abroad in 2025 at about 37% or about 10%; neither is published, both are estimates.454455456466640The annual reports of Swedbank AS, AS SEB Pank, AS LHV Pank and Nets; the ECB's methodological notes on the 2022 reporting change; a request to Eesti Pank.Looked: Eesti Pank's payment statistics and Payments Review of March 2026 (nothing by acquirer); Finantsinspektsioon's review as at 31 December 2025 (shares only among licensed payment and e-money institutions, all payments together); ECB payments statistics; the Competition Authority's decision of 28 March 2022 (online payment solutions only).
5 Apr 2027ExamplesA café's local permits and its till
Which Tallinn city permits does a café need (terrace, signage, opening hours, local alcohol restrictions), and does any act outside those read oblige it to use a cash register?		From the acts read on 5 October 2026: an ordinary café notifies its activity to the Agriculture and Food Board; selling alcohol takes a second notice and a cash register recording every alcohol sale (Alcohol Act §§ 23, 44). No general duty to install one was found in the Taxation Act, whose § 70 lets the tax authority demand one where a taxpayer has not met such a duty imposed by law. The local government - in Tallinn the city - supervises retail sale of alcoholic drinks and its council may restrict it (Alcohol Act §§ 42, 49(4)). No record covers Tallinn's own regulations.501503504524The Tax and Customs Board's guidance on cash sales; Tallinn's regulations in Riigi Teataja and the city's pages for entrepreneurs.Looked: Full-text searches of the Value-Added Tax Act, Taxation Act, Income Tax Act and Consumer Protection Act; the Food Act, the Alcohol Act and the Agriculture and Food Board's page. Not searched: the Accounting Act, the Trading Act, the Tax and Customs Board's guidance, Tallinn city regulations.
5 Apr 2027Founder abroadForeign electronic identities on the company portal
When and under which instrument did the portal stop taking Latvian, Belgian, Finnish and Lithuanian identities for signing; does it take a foreign qualified signature; do its login screen and that of the tax portal e-MTA show the Cyprus eID?		Archived pages of the Centre of Registers show signing with several foreign identities on 5 January 2016 and 18 August 2019, with Estonian and Latvian ID-cards and Estonian and Lithuanian Mobile-ID on 16 January 2021, and Estonian tools only on 2 April 2023; its help page of 12 November 2025 names an Estonian ID-card (e-residency card included), Smart-ID or Mobile-ID. The date and instrument of the change were not found. For login the state service lists Cyprus and the Tax and Customs Board names IDMe.cy; whether the register's signing step takes a foreign qualified signature was not tested.74758081348351The ministerial regulation issued under § 7(2) of the Commercial Register Act, and its predecessor, in their dated versions, or the Centre's release notes; the country list behind the 'EU eID' button in a browser; a test login and a test signature.Looked: Web-archive snapshots of the Centre of Registers' portal page of 5 January 2016, 18 August 2019, 16 January 2021 and 2 April 2023; its help page of 12 November 2025 and portal page of 5 October 2026; the state login service's discovery endpoint; the Tax and Customs Board's page for foreigners (19 February 2026). The login screens need a browser session and were not opened.
5 Apr 2027Founder abroadSocial tax on the fee of a board member insured in Cyprus
How is a board member's mandate classified under Regulation (EC) No 883/2004 when the member lives and is insured in another Member State, what must the Estonian company then do there, and how is the fee coded on the payroll return in force from 5 October 2026?		Per the Tax and Customs Board, social tax is not due in Estonia 'only if' the recipient holds a home-country Form A1; without one the company pays 33%. Regulation (EC) No 883/2004 has no rule specific to company directors, and Article 21 of Regulation (EC) No 987/2009 speaks of employees; the Social Insurance Board's page does not mention board members. Regulation No 21 of the Minister of Finance (in force 5 October 2026) replaced Annex 2 of the return, with its types 156 and 157, by one 'Väljamaksed' part with a field for the A1-issuing state; whether 156 and 157 survive was not established.293312379496641A written position of the Social Insurance Board or the Tax and Customs Board on board members; the Cyprus Social Insurance Services' practice on A1 certificates for directors of foreign companies; the code list of the 2026 form in e-MTA.Looked: The Tax and Customs Board's pages on social tax (20 July 2026) and on e-residents (4 November 2025) and its instructions for Annex 2 of the payroll return (edition from 1 January 2024); Minister of Finance Regulation No 21 of 30 September 2026 (RT I, 02.10.2026, 6); the Social Insurance Board's page on social security abroad (11 March 2026); Regulations 883/2004 and 987/2009. The Cyprus procedure was out of scope.
5 Apr 2027Founder abroadA signature certified in Cyprus
Will the Estonian registrar or a notary accept, in practice, a founder's power of attorney whose signature is certified in Cyprus and carries an apostille?		An application to the registrar must be notarially certified or digitally signed; signatures may also be certified by a foreign official entitled to verify the signatory's identity, and a document certified abroad must be legalised or carry an apostille unless an international agreement provides otherwise (Commercial Register Act § 40(1)). A founder's representative needs a notarially certified power of attorney (Commercial Code § 138(4)). For remote authentication the Chamber of Notaries names only Estonian means of signing. Nothing on documents certified in Cyprus was found.5455350The Chamber of Notaries' guidance on foreign powers of attorney, or a notary's written confirmation.Looked: Commercial Code § 138(4); Commercial Register Act § 40(1)-(2); the Chamber of Notaries' page on remote authentication.
5 Apr 2027RulesEstonian law for the EU anti-money-laundering package
Is there a bill adapting the AML act to Regulation (EU) 2024/1624, which applies from 10 July 2027, and will Estonia set a cash limit below EUR 10,000?		From 10 July 2027 Article 80 of the Regulation caps at EUR 10,000 a cash payment accepted or made by a person trading in goods or providing services; Member States may set lower limits. On 5 October 2026 Estonian law had no general cash ceiling and no lower limit was found. The only two AML-act bills found - bill 640, on the Financial Intelligence Unit's database, and bill 924 SE, on bank-account information - concern other matters. In June 2026 the Ministry of Finance gave the EU's new AML framework and Court of Justice case law as the reason for restricting access to beneficial-owner data.82115137160188A search of the Government's draft-legislation information system (eelnoud.valitsus.ee) for drafts of the Ministry of Finance; the Riigikogu register.Looked: A Riigikogu open-data search of bills with 'rahapesu' in the title on 5 October 2026 (only bills 640 and 924 SE in procedure). The Government's draft-legislation information system and the Ministry of Finance's site were not searched.
5 Apr 2027RulesSector duties to accept cards
Does any sector act or local rule oblige a business in Estonia to accept cards or other electronic payments?		The law obliges a merchant to accept cash - up to 50 coins at once and banknotes without limit, 'unless there is an agreement to use another means of payment' - and no card duty was found in the euro cash-payment act, the Consumer Protection Act or the payment chapters of the Law of Obligations Act. The Alcohol Act (§ 20(1)) and the Tobacco Act (§ 20) require non-cash settlement in the import, wholesale and export of alcohol and the wholesale of tobacco products. The Public Transport Act sets no means of payment for taxis; the banks' offline-payment duty binds no merchant.186188528529A search of the remaining sector acts and of Tallinn's rules in Riigi Teataja; the Consumer Protection and Technical Regulatory Authority's guidance for traders.Looked: Keyword searches of the Estonian texts of the euro cash-payment act, the Consumer Protection Act and the payment chapters of the Law of Obligations Act; Alcohol Act § 20(1) and Tobacco Act § 20; Public Transport Act § 65 - on 5 October 2026. Other sector acts and local-government rules were not searched.
5 Apr 2027StagesWhere the share capital sits before registration
Where does a founder who cannot open an account for the company being founded hold a cash contribution of up to EUR 50,000, and does an account at an e-money institution satisfy the Commercial Code?		The Commercial Code names a payment account for the company being founded at an EEA credit or payment institution (§ 520(4), wording of 1 January 2019) or, in the expedited procedure, a deposit with the registrar or a start-up account under AML act § 27(2), whose contribution must arrive through an account at an EEA credit institution. Up to EUR 50,000 the board members only confirm payment in the application. The text of 5 October 2026 names payment institutions, not e-money institutions. Outline assembled from the records: no source read says what a founder does who cannot open one.105314326335642The explanatory memoranda to the Acts RT I, 20.12.2018, 1 and RT I, 05.05.2022, 1; written guidance of the registrar or of the Ministry of Justice and Digital Affairs on which accounts are accepted; a registrar ruling.Looked: Commercial Code §§ 140-144 and 520; Commercial Register Act § 42; AML act § 27(2); the Centre of Registers' establishment guide (27 October 2025); the e-Residency programme's articles on founding and on banking.
5 Apr 2027TaxesThe treaty's dividend article and the company-level tax
How does Estonia apply Article 10(1) of the Estonia-Cyprus convention to the income tax a company pays on distributed profit when the shareholder is a Cyprus resident?		Article 10(1) says dividends paid by a company of one state to a resident of the other 'shall be taxable only in that other State'; the convention has no rate table for dividends. Under the Income Tax Act a dividend is taxed once, at company level, and nothing is withheld from the shareholder, resident or non-resident, apart from one transitional 7% withholding. No record carries an authority's statement on how the article bears on the company-level tax.254257313An explanation by the Tax and Customs Board or the Ministry of Finance of treaty dividend articles and § 50 of the Income Tax Act, or Estonian case law.Looked: The English original and the Estonian translation of the convention (Articles 10 and 21); the Tax and Customs Board's page for e-residents.
5 Apr 2027TaxesVAT registration when the only foreign service is exempt
Must an Estonian company that is not VAT-registered register as a taxable person with limited liability when the only service it receives from abroad is an exempt payment service, for example from an acquirer established in another member state?		Under § 21(1) of the Value-Added Tax Act an Estonian person receiving from a foreign business not registered for VAT in Estonia a service listed in § 10(5) - financial services among them - must register as a 'taxable person with limited liability' from the day the service is received and pays the VAT on such services itself. § 16(2^1) exempts payment services within the meaning of the Payment Institutions and E-money Institutions Act; the Act does not name merchant acquiring as such. No text read says whether the duty to register arises when the service received is exempt.271557The Board's VAT handbook chapter on persons with limited VAT liability, or a written reply.Looked: Value-Added Tax Act § 3(5), § 10(5), § 16(2^1) and § 21; the Tax and Customs Board's page 'Registration as VAT payer'.
5 Apr 2027TaxesTax refunds to an account outside an Estonian bank
Does the Tax and Customs Board in practice refund overpaid tax to an IBAN at a payment or e-money institution, and can e-MTA initiate a payment from an account outside Estonia?		As of 5 October 2026 Estonian tax law does not tie tax payment to an Estonian bank account. The Board's page 'Payment of taxes' (updated 21 July 2026) has taxes paid by bank transfer to its accounts at SEB Pank, Swedbank, Luminor Bank, LHV Pank and Coop Pank, or in e-MTA by credit card or bank link. For refunds Taxation Act § 106 (text in force from 1 October 2026) asks only for 'the taxpayer's bank account details' and foresees an account abroad, the Board then being allowed to deduct the transfer costs (§ 106(4)). No record covers the Board's practice with e-money accounts.643The Board's written guidance on refund accounts, or a test by a taxpayer.Looked: The Board's page 'Payment of taxes' (updated 21 July 2026) and Taxation Act §§ 105-106, on 5 October 2026.
5 Apr 2027TimelineShare of non-residents in banking: three figures, no definition
What do Finantsinspektsioon's shares of non-residents measure, and why do they differ from the shares computed from Eesti Pank's table?		Finantsinspektsioon's annual reports give 19.1% at end-2014, 16.2% in 2015, 11.7% in 2017, 7.9% at end-2018 and 10.0% at end-2019, called 'non-resident deposits' for 2017 and 2018 and non-resident clients or 'non-residents in banking' elsewhere. Its web page gives 20% for 2014 and 7.9% for end-2018 and does not say whether they are shares of deposits, of clients or of turnover. Computed in this study from Eesti Pank's year-end deposit table: 18.6% for 2014, 7.2% for 2018. Eesti Pank, counting only foreign non-financial companies and households, put it at a peak of 21% in 2012 and 8.5% by 2017.158568644A methodological note from Finantsinspektsioon, or its supervisory reporting template.Looked: Finantsinspektsioon's page 'Countering Money Laundering' (last edited 19 March 2019) and its annual reports and yearbooks for 2015-2019; Eesti Pank's table 'Stock of deposits by customer group, residence, currency and maturity'.
Method · How this page was built

Read in the primary document, attacked by a second reader, stored word for word

The page is a view of 590 memory records of one round, beside the Cyprus records its comparison cites. Each record is one checked statement with its source, and the page says nothing that a record does not carry.

Order of work. On 5 October 2026 the round put the reader's eight Cyprus questions to Estonia, with one more about the founder who does not live there, in seven research slices: formation, the register and yearly duties; bank accounts, AML and KYC; acquiring and the payments market; taxes; providers and two walk-throughs; timeline and numbers; the founder abroad. Each slice was read by two adversarial verifiers: of 490 researcher facts 253 were confirmed, 237 corrected and none refuted; the verifiers added 108.

From records to page. The 590 records went into memory in 30 batches, each compared with the prepared file byte by byte: 590 of 590 identical. Eleven section drafts were written from the records the same day. The work stopped there and resumed on 8 October 2026, when every draft not yet checked was checked item by item against its records. Together with the one section checked on 5 October, 218 of 555 items were corrected, none removed and 54 added. As on the Cyprus pages, few statements were wrong outright: most had lost a qualifier, carried a negative wider than the search behind it, or showed an earlier version of a document as the current one. The comparison section is four parts written separately and joined here.

Records stored once. The appendix lists each cited record by the publisher of the document it was read in, the date it was read and a link to that document. The record itself, with its full text, its source and the pages that cite it, is kept once, on the Estonia records page or, for the Cyprus side of the comparison, the Cyprus records page; a reference number shows the record's text on hover.

Limits. This is a reference study, not legal, immigration or tax advice. Prices are providers' own published prices on the dates given, and plans include different things; times are what an authority or a provider publishes; nothing is added up into a total. A statement that something is not published holds only for the places and the date named.

Records · What each number points to

644 memory records are cited above

Each number points to one record in gid's memory: 513 Estonian, 131 from the Cyprus rounds. Here each is listed by the publisher of the document it was read in, the date it was read and a link to that document; the record itself - its full text, its source and the other pages that cite it - is kept once, on the Estonia records page or the Cyprus records page. Hover or focus a number to read the record.
